diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-device/tests/reference_actions.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-device/tests/reference_actions.rs
index 06934c8233..8f724f137e 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-device/tests/reference_actions.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-device/tests/reference_actions.rs
@@ -77,6 +77,42 @@ fn task_ids<S: DeviceStore>(d: &mut Device<S>) -> Json {
 }
 
 fn run<H: Host>(host: &mut H) {
+    // Server expiry is a physical retirement with the schema's declared
+    // referential effects. Devices receive that one committed closure just as
+    // they receive an authored parent delete.
+    let expiring = r#"
+table parents:
+  until: time
+  expire at .until
+  public 'parents'
+  sync public
+table cascadeChildren:
+  parent: parents on delete cascade
+  public 'cascadeChildren'
+  sync public
+table nullableChildren:
+  parent: parents ? on delete set null
+  public 'nullableChildren'
+  sync public
+"#;
+    let (mut e, b) = compile(expiring);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("parents".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("p")), ("until", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("cascadeChildren".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c")), ("parent", id("p"))])),
+        ("nullableChildren".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("n")), ("parent", id("p"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let mut d = host.open("expiry-actions", Some(b)).unwrap();
+    acquire(&mut e, &mut d, "alice", 0);
+    let from = d.state().unwrap().0;
+    assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 100).unwrap(), 1);
+    d.apply(&stream_page(&mut e, "alice", from), false).unwrap();
+    assert!(d.store().raw_rows("parents").is_empty());
+    assert!(d.store().raw_rows("cascadeChildren").is_empty());
+    let nullable = d.store().raw_rows("nullableChildren");
+    assert_eq!(nullable.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(nullable[0].field("parent").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Null);
+    host.close("expiry-actions", d);
+
     // Every referencing group is public and held: the cascade predicts, deleting the held tasks.
     let (mut e, b) = compile(PUBLIC);
     assert_eq!(write(&mut e, "setup", "alice", &[]), "ok");
@@ -173,6 +209,55 @@ mutation drop(id: parents):
     host.close("retained-cascade", d);
 }
 
+#[test]
+fn authoritative_memory_sweeps_resume_and_wrap_past_restricted_roots() {
+    let source = r#"
+table roots:
+  expiresAt: time ?
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public 'roots'
+table cascadeKids:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'cascade kids'
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'nullable kids'
+table blockers:
+  root: roots
+  public 'blockers'
+"#;
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let mut store = snapback4_device::memory::MemoryStore::new();
+    store.activate_server_at(bound.schema, 0).unwrap();
+    store.seed_server_at([
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("a-blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("b-blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c-blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("z-free")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("cz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("nz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("ba")), ("root", id("a-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("bb")), ("root", id("b-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("bc")), ("root", id("c-blocked"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let wrapped_removed: u64 = (0..4).map(|_| store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap()).sum();
+    assert_eq!(wrapped_removed, 1, "continuation reaches the free root within one bounded turn per due root");
+    assert!(store.raw_rows("cascadeKids").is_empty());
+    assert_eq!(store.raw_rows("nullableKids")[0].field("root").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Null);
+
+    // The cursor is after z. A lower new key is found after the scan wraps;
+    // each restricted root still consumes one bounded visit.
+    store.seed_server_at([("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", id("aa-free")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10)),
+    ]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let wrapped_removed: u64 = (0..4).map(|_| store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap()).sum();
+    assert_eq!(wrapped_removed, 1, "a wrapped scan reaches the new lower key within one bounded turn per due root");
+    assert!(!store.raw_rows("roots").iter().any(|row| row.field("id").unwrap() == &id("aa-free")));
+}
+
 #[test] fn reference_actions_memory() { run(&mut Memory::new()); }
 #[test] fn reference_actions_sqlite() { run(&mut Sqlite::new("reference-actions")); }
 
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-sim/tests/expiry_reference_actions.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-sim/tests/expiry_reference_actions.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..05a91f6ddf
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-sim/tests/expiry_reference_actions.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,140 @@
+//! Expiry is a physical delete trigger for referential actions (LLP 3000.039
+//! §3). These cases cover scheduled cleanup, commit-end retirement and
+//! same-id replacement of an expired row against the real engine.
+use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, value::object};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+
+fn id(value: &str) -> Value { Value::Id(value.into()) }
+
+fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap()
+}
+
+const SWEEP: &str = r#"
+table roots:
+  expiresAt: time ?
+  by byExpiry: expiresAt
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public 'roots'
+  sync public
+table cascadeKids:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'cascade kids'
+  sync public
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'nullable kids'
+  sync public
+table blockers:
+  root: roots
+  public 'blockers'
+  sync public
+"#;
+
+#[test]
+fn sweep_applies_every_action_and_advances_past_a_restricted_root() {
+    let mut e = engine(SWEEP);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("a-blocked")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("b-blocked")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("c-blocked")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("z-free")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), object([("id", id("ca")), ("root", id("a-blocked"))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), object([("id", id("cz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), object([("id", id("nz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), object([("id", id("ba")), ("root", id("a-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), object([("id", id("bb")), ("root", id("b-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), object([("id", id("bc")), ("root", id("c-blocked"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+
+    for expected in [0, 0, 0, 1] {
+        assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 1).unwrap(), expected, "a one-row sweep must advance its physical scan position");
+    }
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().iter().map(|r| r.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec!["a-blocked", "b-blocked", "c-blocked"]);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().iter().map(|r| r.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec!["ca"]);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("nullableKids").unwrap()[0].field("root").unwrap(), &Value::Null);
+
+    for blocker in ["ba", "bb", "bc"] { e.seed_delete("blockers", blocker).unwrap(); }
+    assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 10).unwrap(), 3);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().is_empty());
+    assert!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().is_empty());
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn a_late_cascade_refusal_rolls_back_earlier_set_null_writes() {
+    let source = r#"
+table roots:
+  expiresAt: time ?
+  by byExpiry: expiresAt
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public 'roots'
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 1 by root
+  public 'nullable kids'
+table children:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 1 by root
+  public 'children'
+table grandchildren:
+  child: children
+  read through .child
+"#;
+    let mut e = engine(source);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("p")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), object([("id", id("n")), ("root", id("p"))])),
+        ("children".into(), object([("id", id("c")), ("root", id("p"))])),
+        ("grandchildren".into(), object([("id", id("g")), ("child", id("c"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+
+    let error = e.sweep_expired(10, 10).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(error.code(), "E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN", "{error:?}");
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("nullableKids").unwrap()[0].field("root").unwrap(), &id("p"), "the prior set-null write rolled back");
+    for table in ["roots", "children", "grandchildren"] {
+        assert_eq!(e.store().rows(table).unwrap().len(), 1, "{table}");
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn commit_retirement_and_expired_same_id_replacement_apply_actions() {
+    let source = format!(r#"{SWEEP}
+mutation bornExpired():
+  insert roots {{id: 'born', expiresAt: now}}
+  insert cascadeKids {{id: 'born-c', root: 'born'}}
+  insert nullableKids {{id: 'born-n', root: 'born'}}
+  return null
+mutation bornRestricted():
+  insert roots {{id: 'blocked', expiresAt: now}}
+  insert blockers {{id: 'blocked-b', root: 'blocked'}}
+  return null
+mutation replaceExpired():
+  insert roots {{id: 'old', expiresAt: null}}
+  return null
+"#);
+    let mut e = engine(&source);
+    let sent = e.mutate("bornExpired", None, Context::new("dev:alice", BTreeMap::new()).at(100)).unwrap();
+    assert!(matches!(sent.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{:?}", sent.state);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().is_empty());
+    assert!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().is_empty());
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("nullableKids").unwrap()[0].field("root").unwrap(), &Value::Null);
+
+    let refused = e.mutate("bornRestricted", None, Context::new("dev:alice", BTreeMap::new()).at(100)).unwrap();
+    assert!(matches!(refused.state, WriteState::Failed { ref why, .. } if why.code == "E_REFERENCED"), "{:?}", refused.state);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().is_empty(), "the refused birth and its blocker roll back together");
+    assert!(e.store().rows("blockers").unwrap().is_empty());
+
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("old")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), object([("id", id("old-c")), ("root", id("old"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let replaced = e.mutate("replaceExpired", None, Context::new("dev:alice", BTreeMap::new()).at(100)).unwrap();
+    assert!(matches!(replaced.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{:?}", replaced.state);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap()[0].field("expiresAt").unwrap(), &Value::Null);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().is_empty(), "same-id cleanup cannot orphan the expired predecessor's child");
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/aggregate_identity_upgrade.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/aggregate_identity_upgrade.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..c6754a981f
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/aggregate_identity_upgrade.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,175 @@
+//! A store written by the frozen 0.4.28 binary adopts digest-addressed
+//! aggregate rows before the current engine serves it.
+use rusqlite::Connection;
+use std::{
+    fs,
+    path::{Path, PathBuf},
+    process::Command,
+};
+
+const SOURCE: &str = r#"use personas alice
+table entries:
+  group: text <=256
+  amount: int
+  insert <- allow
+  public
+view totals: sum entries of .amount by .group
+  public
+mutation add(group: text <=256, amount: int):
+  insert entries {group, amount}
+query total(group: text <=256):
+  return totals[group]
+"#;
+
+struct Temp(PathBuf);
+impl Temp {
+    fn new() -> Self {
+        let path = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
+            "snapback4-maintained-id-{}-{}",
+            std::process::id(),
+            std::time::SystemTime::now()
+                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
+                .unwrap()
+                .as_nanos()
+        ));
+        fs::create_dir_all(path.join("snapback")).unwrap();
+        fs::write(path.join("snapback/schema.q"), SOURCE).unwrap();
+        Self(path)
+    }
+}
+impl Drop for Temp {
+    fn drop(&mut self) {
+        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
+    }
+}
+
+fn aggregate_id(store: &Path) -> String {
+    Connection::open(store)
+        .unwrap()
+        .query_row(
+            "SELECT r.id FROM r JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=r.t WHERE c.name='totals'",
+            [],
+            |row| row.get(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap()
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore = "requires frozen 0.4.28 binary via SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN"]
+fn a_0_4_28_store_rebuilds_legacy_aggregate_ids_and_fences_old_devices() {
+    let baseline = PathBuf::from(
+        std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN")
+            .expect("set SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN to the frozen 0.4.28 snapback4 binary"),
+    );
+    assert!(
+        baseline.is_file(),
+        "SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN is not a file: {}",
+        baseline.display()
+    );
+    let version = Command::new(&baseline).arg("--version").output().unwrap();
+    assert!(version.status.success());
+    assert!(
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&version.stdout).contains("0.4.28"),
+        "unexpected baseline: {:?}",
+        version
+    );
+
+    let dir = Temp::new();
+    let group = "x".repeat(200);
+    let args = serde_json::json!({"group": group, "amount": 7}).to_string();
+    let written = Command::new(&baseline)
+        .current_dir(&dir.0)
+        .args(["run", "add", &args, "--as", "alice", "--now", "1", "--json"])
+        .output()
+        .unwrap();
+    assert!(
+        written.status.success(),
+        "{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&written.stderr)
+    );
+    let store = dir.0.join(".snapback4/store.sqlite");
+    let legacy = aggregate_id(&store);
+    assert!(
+        legacy.len() > snapback4_core::rows::MAX_ID_BYTES,
+        "the frozen binary must have written the oversized legacy address"
+    );
+    let before: u64 = Connection::open(&store)
+        .unwrap()
+        .query_row(
+            "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='generation'",
+            [],
+            |row| row.get::<_, String>(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap()
+        .parse()
+        .unwrap();
+
+    let opened = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4"))
+        .current_dir(&dir.0)
+        .args([
+            "run",
+            "total",
+            &serde_json::json!({"group": group}).to_string(),
+            "--as",
+            "alice",
+            "--now",
+            "2",
+            "--json",
+        ])
+        .output()
+        .unwrap();
+    assert!(
+        opened.status.success(),
+        "stdout={} stderr={}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&opened.stdout),
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&opened.stderr)
+    );
+    let answer: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_slice(&opened.stdout).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(answer["data"]["value"], 7, "{answer}");
+
+    let digest = aggregate_id(&store);
+    assert_eq!(digest.len(), 64);
+    assert_ne!(digest, legacy);
+    let conn = Connection::open(&store).unwrap();
+    let (after, marker): (u64, String) = (
+        conn.query_row(
+            "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='generation'",
+            [],
+            |row| row.get::<_, String>(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap()
+        .parse()
+        .unwrap(),
+        conn.query_row(
+            "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key=?1",
+            [snapback4_core::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META],
+            |row| row.get(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap(),
+    );
+    assert!(
+        after > before,
+        "identity adoption must publish a new device generation"
+    );
+    assert_eq!(marker, snapback4_core::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION);
+
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), SOURCE.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let required =
+        snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&bound.schema.public_projection());
+    assert!(
+        required
+            .capabilities
+            .contains(&"maintained-id-sha256-v1".to_owned())
+    );
+    let mut old = snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current();
+    old.version = "0.4.28".into();
+    old.capabilities
+        .retain(|capability| capability != "maintained-id-sha256-v1");
+    let refusal = snapback4_core::device_protocol::check(Some(&old), &required).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_DEVICE_VERSION");
+    assert!(
+        refusal.message.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"),
+        "{refusal:?}"
+    );
+}

# LLP 3000: Snapback 4 — the decided-substrates candidate

**Type:** Explainer
**Status:** Active
**Role:** Root of the Snapback 4 corpus (LLPs 3000 and up)
**Systems:** Everything under `snapback4/` once it exists; the bakeoff protocol shared with Snapback 3 (LLP 2000) and Snapback 2 (LLP 1000)
**Author:** Charlie Cheever / Claude Fable 5.1
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-13 (FLAKE: isolate real-timer client fixtures and keep quote layout pooled); 2026-10-13 (§7: timed-out compiler-group inspector retries within the unchanged drain deadline); 2026-10-12 (§7: a reference declares what deleting its row does, LLP 3000.039); 2026-10-12 (§7: `file` assets, LLP 3000.038); 2026-10-08 (§7: S3 long-wait capacity — sized viewer and claim pools, per-principal share, `E_STREAMS`, shared change answers, per-ticket engine wakeups); 2026-10-08 (§7: FL asynchronous initial identity capture and independent owned cleanup); 2026-10-07 (§7: FL fault dispatch checks exact birth and command); 2026-10-06 (§7: PD stale-enumeration regression distinguishes Darwin identity rereads); 2026-10-06 (§7: PS Round 2 asynchronous inspection and completed-snapshot cleanup deadlines); 2026-10-05 (§7: PW immutable browser fixtures and navigation diagnostics); 2026-10-04 (§7: NZ integration, bounded release-command diagnostic drain); 2026-10-04 (§7: LU round 2, adaptive delivery lookahead and repayment of empty-kind CPU); 2026-10-04 (§7: QF2 bounded fresh snapshots for exiting routing anomalies); 2026-10-04 (§7: LU round 1, durable pending-item share under continuing arrivals); 2026-10-03 (§7: LU rotates bounded Following turns between pending items); 2026-10-03 (§7: QF Round 9, fresh Darwin identity reads retain file scope); 2026-10-03 (§7: QF Round 8, signed ps UIDs and ownership-scoped metadata validation); 2026-10-03 (§7: QF Round 6, process-group-only custody and Node result semantics); 2026-10-03 (§7: QF Round 4, vanished PIDs are exited; retain exact malformed metadata and the first error); 2026-10-03 (§7: QF Round 3, unreadable candidates are diagnostic-only on shared hosts); 2026-10-03 (§7: QF round 2 and Grok follow-up, group/session/token custody and a subtest-progress watchdog); 2026-10-02 (§7: QF round 1, retained process groups, fail-closed inspection and guard-aware file caps); ## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

The merged 20k Lean campaign found three new completeness disagreements in
simple party pages pinning their leading order column. PAGE4's general positive
classifier bypassed the existing simple classifier's system/party order check.
Apply that same physical-order requirement to the independent compound model:
the authored native scan stays supported and incomplete, including empty and
nullable-order cases; do not hide the cases behind an Unsupported label.
Two model regressions retain readable rows and unknown absence. No runtime,
compiler plan, read authority or device bytes change. Re-run the complete 20k
campaign and formal checks after this model correction.

### 2026-10-14 — FIX28: pre-wave defects in unpublished 0.4.28

Keep 0.4.28. Extend both packed proof and starter-toolchain qualification to
every supported frontend/template pair, twice; require their complete matrices
in imported Linux evidence. Browser execution belongs to the Linux host; clean
Ubuntu/Alpine userspaces still independently build all packed starters. Default
web/Expo adaptation also runs twice. The expanded baseline run exposes web chat
ignoring ?api= as well as the reported Expo chat/social clients; all current
clients now use the Todos runtime URL override. Historical byte templates stay
historical. Generated READMEs explain both proofs, runtime ranges and CHROME.
Native smoke uses the runtime launcher, reading its current range on each run.

Runtime history acknowledgment is independent of successful compiler snapshots.
First check delivers history once even on refusal; upgrade baseline checks cannot
consume it, and install-first cannot overwrite older compiler facts. Refuse an
implicit downgrade before compilation/install, with an explicit --to remedy.
Human upgrade runs human check; --json emits one object and puts progress/history
on stderr. LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and its packaged-proof child record this.

Dormant row populations cover their own retained cells, bytes and assets; only
active rows contribute standalone column cells/samples. The archived-row example
is one row plus two cells. Audit keeps actual restoration counters and distinguishes
a truly empty definition from zero standalone cells covered by a dormant row.
The widening note says rows that later use those values. LLP 3000.000.000 §11
and the deploy card document the disjoint counts. Validation/review follows.

check regressions verify each stable history entry once after prereleases;

-/// The id of a maintained row is its group's canonical form, as Snapback 2
-/// spelled it: an aggregate's identity is its key, and the conformance
-/// corpus holds it to this spelling. Programs read aggregates by group,
-/// never by id.
+/// Activation marker for the maintained-row address function. A missing or
+/// older marker rebuilds every aggregate target before the generation opens.
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_META: &str = "maintained_id_version";
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION: &str = "sha256-v1";
+
+/// The id of a maintained row is a fixed-size digest of its typed group key.
+/// Programs address aggregates by their group index, never by this internal
+/// id. Domain separation makes future encodings explicit migrations.
+// @ref LLP 3000.007.003#30-aggregate-target-identity-and-migration — every aggregate writer, synthetic check row and adoption rebuild shares this bounded identity.
 pub fn maintained_id(key: &[Value]) -> String {
-    crate::value::canonical_json(&key)
+    crate::value::digest(&("snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1", key))
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn maintained_identity_is_fixed_size_and_domain_separated() {
+    let short = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".into())]);
+    let long = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".repeat(100_000))]);
+    assert_eq!(short.len(), 64);
+    assert_eq!(long.len(), 64);
+    assert_ne!(short, long);
+    assert_ne!(short, crate::value::digest(&[Value::String("x".into())]));
 }
 
 /// Whether a value is (or contains) the reserved system principal.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/adoption_tests/retirement.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/adoption_tests/retirement.rs
index eb269bcd9d..1221771d7c 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/adoption_tests/retirement.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/adoption_tests/retirement.rs
@@ -92,6 +92,67 @@ fn rebuild_groups(next: &str, remaining: usize) {
     e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 10).unwrap();
     assert_eq!(e.store().rows("totals").unwrap().len(), remaining);
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn missing_identity_marker_rebuilds_a_principal_reference_and_max_text_group() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table parents:\n  public\ntable entries:\n  owner: principal\n  parent: parents\n  token: text <=64\n  amount: int\n  public\nview totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    let owner = format!("user:{}", "o".repeat(59));
+    let parent = "p".repeat(64);
+    let token = "t".repeat(64);
+    assert_eq!((owner.len(), parent.len(), token.len()), (64, 64, 64));
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("parents".into(), object([("id", Value::Id(parent.clone()))])),
+        ("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("owner", Value::Id(owner.clone())),
+            ("parent", Value::Id(parent.clone())), ("token", Value::String(token.clone())), ("amount", Value::Int(7))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Id(owner), Value::Id(parent), Value::String(token)]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+    assert_eq!(expected.len(), 64);
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META).unwrap().as_deref(), Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION));
+    let after = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));

+The SQLite metadata and evaluation-PostgreSQL backend marker
+`maintained_id_version = sha256-v1` bind persisted aggregate targets to that
+function. Opening or adopting either store without the marker — including a
+0.4.28 store whose ids were the unbounded canonical JSON of the group key —
+atomically clears and rebuilds every ordinary aggregate target from its source
+rows, writes the marker, and publishes a new semantic generation. A failure
+rolls back old rows, rebuilt rows, marker and generation together. Adoption
+advice counts the SQLite rebuild even when schema/program bytes are unchanged;
+the evaluation adapter performs the corresponding rebuild under its opening
+writer/head fence. Schemas with ordinary maintains require device capability
 `maintained-id-sha256-v1`; an older device is refused at the handshake before
 it can compute a legacy id and silently read a digest-addressed aggregate as
 zero.

+actions in one transaction (LLP 3000.039 §3). A live restrict child or a

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/postgres/maintained_identity_upgrade.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/postgres/maintained_identity_upgrade.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..3708ad4a5a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/postgres/maintained_identity_upgrade.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,49 @@
+//! Atomic adoption of the maintained-row address function in the evaluation
+//! adapter. The marker and generation publish in the same writer transaction
+//! as the target rebuild, so no writer can observe mixed legacy/digest ids.
+use super::*;
+
+pub(super) fn install(
+    tx: &store::Tx<'_>,
+    schema: &Schema,
+    creating: bool,
+    generation: u64,
+) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
+    let current = tx.query(
+        "SELECT maintained_identity_version FROM _backend WHERE singleton",
+        &[],
+    )?[0].get::<_, Option<&str>>(0).map(str::to_owned);
+    if current.as_deref() == Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION) {
+        return Ok(generation);
+    }
+
+    let aggregates: Vec<_> = schema.maintains.iter()
+        .filter(|maintain| maintain.private.is_none() && maintain.row_key.is_none())
+        .cloned()
+        .collect();
+    if !creating && !aggregates.is_empty() {
+        let mut runner = Runner::new(
+            tx,
+            schema,
+            Context::system().at(crate::now_ms()),
+            Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort),
+            tx.next_seq()?,
+        );
+        runner.event_free = true;
+        for maintain in &aggregates {
+            runner.rebuild_maintain(maintain)?;
+        }
+    }
+
+    let generation = if creating || aggregates.is_empty() {
+        generation
+    } else {
+        generation.checked_add(1)
+            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))?
+    };
+    tx.execute(
+        "UPDATE _backend SET maintained_identity_version=$1, generation=$2 WHERE singleton",
+        &[&crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION, &store::signed(generation)?],
+    )?;
+    Ok(generation)
+}

+#[test]
+#[ignore = "requires isolated local Postgres; scripts/test-postgres.sh"]
+fn reopening_legacy_maintained_ids_rebuilds_before_publishing_the_generation() {
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), r#"
+table entries:
+  bucket: int
+  public
+view totals: count entries by .bucket
+  public
+"#.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let schema: Schema = serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&bound.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
+    let programs = bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+    let url = std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES").expect("private test-postgres.sh database");
+    let name = format!("sb4_eval_maintained_id_{:x}", crate::ids::seed());
+    let mut engine = PostgresEngine::create(Client::connect(&url, postgres::NoTls).unwrap(), &name, schema, programs).unwrap();
+    engine.load([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("bucket", Value::Int(7))]))]).unwrap();
+    let digest = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let legacy = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let target = engine.schema.tables["totals"].clone();
+    let generation = engine.generation();
+    let tx = engine.store.transaction(Some(WriteMode::Serialized)).unwrap();
+    let old = tx.get("totals", &digest).unwrap().expect("current aggregate");
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &old).unwrap();
+    let mut old = old;
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut old {
+        fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(legacy.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("totals", &target, &old, None).unwrap();
+    tx.execute("UPDATE _backend SET maintained_identity_version=NULL WHERE singleton", &[]).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    drop(engine);
+
+    let mut reopened = PostgresEngine::open(Client::connect(&url, postgres::NoTls).unwrap(), &name).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(reopened.generation(), generation + 1);
+    let tx = reopened.store.transaction(None).unwrap();
+    assert!(tx.get("totals", &legacy).unwrap().is_none());
+    let rebuilt = tx.get("totals", &digest).unwrap().expect("digest aggregate");
+    assert_eq!(rebuilt.field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+

         "items": {"columns": {"id": "Id", "key": {"Optional": {"Text": {"max": 100}}}},

@@ -8,6 +8,30 @@ use crate::{
 };
 use std::collections::BTreeMap;
 
+const EXPIRY_TABLE_CURSOR: &str = "$expiry-table";
+
+/// Rotate a sweep's table order after the last table that consumed a bounded
+/// visit. Per-table row cursors alone can starve every later table when an
+/// earlier table contains a permanently restricted due row.
+pub(crate) fn resume_expiry_tables(
+    mut tables: Vec<String>,
+    cursors: &BTreeMap<String, Vec<Value>>,
+) -> Vec<String> {
+    let after = cursors
+        .get(EXPIRY_TABLE_CURSOR)
+        .and_then(|cursor| cursor.first())
+        .and_then(|value| value.as_str().ok());
+    if let Some(index) = after.and_then(|after| tables.iter().position(|table| table == after)) {
+        let next = (index + 1) % tables.len().max(1);
+        tables.rotate_left(next);
+    }
+    tables
+}
+
+pub(crate) fn mark_expiry_table(cursors: &mut BTreeMap<String, Vec<Value>>, table: &str) {
+    cursors.insert(EXPIRY_TABLE_CURSOR.into(), vec![Value::String(table.into())]);
+}

+    // A read-through child below a cascade is a physical-retention blocker.
+    // The server preflights it before set-null writes and continues to a later
+    // due root; devices receive only that committed result.
+    let retained_expiry = r#"
+table roots:
+  until: time
+  expire at .until
+  public
+  sync public
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 1 by root
+  public
+  sync public
+table children:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 1 by root
+  public
+  sync public
+table grandchildren:
+  child: children
+  immutable child
+  read through .child
+"#;
+    let (mut e, b) = compile(retained_expiry);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("p")), ("until", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("z")), ("until", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("n")), ("root", id("p"))])),
+        ("children".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c")), ("root", id("p"))])),
+        ("grandchildren".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("g")), ("child", id("c"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let mut d = host.open("expiry-retained-cascade", Some(b)).unwrap();
+    acquire(&mut e, &mut d, "alice", 0);
+    let from = d.state().unwrap().0;
+    assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 10).unwrap(), 1);
+    d.apply(&stream_page(&mut e, "alice", from), false).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(d.store().raw_rows("roots").len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(d.store().raw_rows("nullableKids")[0].field("root").unwrap(), &id("p"));
+    assert_eq!(d.store().raw_rows("children").len(), 1);
+    host.close("expiry-retained-cascade", d);
+
     // Every referencing group is public and held: the cascade predicts, deleting the held tasks.
     let (mut e, b) = compile(PUBLIC);
     assert_eq!(write(&mut e, "setup", "alice", &[]), "ok");
@@ -298,6 +351,34 @@ table blockers:
     let wrapped_removed: u64 = (0..4).map(|_| store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap()).sum();
     assert_eq!(wrapped_removed, 1, "a wrapped scan reaches the new lower key within one bounded turn per due root");
     assert!(!store.raw_rows("roots").iter().any(|row| row.field("id").unwrap() == &id("aa-free")));
+
+    // Row continuation is insufficient on its own: a permanently blocked
+    // table must yield the next bounded call to later expiry tables.
+    let source = r#"
+table aFree:
+  expiresAt: time
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public
+table zBlocked:
+  expiresAt: time
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public
+table zReferences:
+  root: zBlocked on delete restrict
+  public
+"#;
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let mut store = snapback4_device::memory::MemoryStore::new();
+    store.activate_server_at(bound.schema, 0).unwrap();
+    store.seed_server_at([
+        ("zBlocked".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("zReferences".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("witness")), ("root", id("blocked"))])),
+        ("aFree".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("free")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap(), 1, "the table cursor advances past a blocked expiry table");
+    assert!(store.raw_rows("aFree").is_empty());
 }

+/// The copied corpus is a frozen image of the pre-digest store. Translate
+/// maintained target identities at the adapter boundary so its logical values
+/// remain an oracle for the current address function. Other row ids retain
+/// their exact frozen spelling.
+fn legacy_maintained_ids(schema: &Schema, rows: &[ReceiptRow]) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, String>, String> {
+    let mut ids = BTreeMap::new();
+    for receipt in rows {
+        let Some(maintain) = schema.maintains.iter().find(|maintain| maintain.target == receipt.table && maintain.row_key.is_none()) else { continue };
+        let old = match receipt.row.field("id") {
+            Ok(Value::Id(id)) => id,
+            Ok(_) => return Err(format!("{}.id is not an id", receipt.table)),
+            Err(error) => return Err(format!("{}.id: {error}", receipt.table)),
+        };
+        let key = maintain.by.iter().map(|field| receipt.row.field(field).cloned().map_err(|error| format!("{}.{}: {error}", receipt.table, field))).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
+        let new = snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&key);
+        if let Some(prior) = ids.insert(old.clone(), new.clone())
+            && prior != new
+        {
+            return Err(format!("legacy id {old} names more than one maintained group"));
+        }
+    }
+    Ok(ids)
+}
+
+fn translate_legacy_maintained_row(schema: &Schema, table: &str, row: &mut Value, ids: &BTreeMap<String, String>) {
+    if !schema.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.target == table && maintain.row_key.is_none()) { return; }
+    let (Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields)) = row else { return };
+    let Some(Value::Id(id)) = fields.get_mut("id") else { return };
+    if let Some(replacement) = ids.get(id) {
+        *id = replacement.clone();
+    }
+}
+
+fn translate_legacy_maintained_expected(expected: &mut Expected, ids: &BTreeMap<String, String>) -> Result<(), String> {
+    if ids.is_empty() || expected.error_code.is_some() { return Ok(()); }
+    let Some(canonical) = &expected.canonical_result else { return Ok(()) };
+    let mut value: Value = serde_json::from_str(canonical).map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
+    translate_legacy_maintained_result(&mut value, ids);
+    expected.canonical_result = Some(snapback4_core::value::canonical_json(&value));
+    Ok(())
+}
+
+fn translate_legacy_maintained_result(value: &mut Value, ids: &BTreeMap<String, String>) {
+    match value {
+        Value::Id(id) => {
+            if let Some(replacement) = ids.get(id) {
+                *id = replacement.clone();
+            }
+        }
+        Value::Array(values) => values.iter_mut().for_each(|value| translate_legacy_maintained_result(value, ids)),
+        Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) => fields.values_mut().for_each(|value| translate_legacy_maintained_result(value, ids)),
+        _ => {}
+    }
+}
+
 /// The schema validator exposes a path/message instead of SB2's code.
 /// Translate only this observed, typed admission category. A schema that
 /// unexpectedly starts validating still runs and fails its refusal oracle.
@@ -796,7 +859,7 @@ mod adapter {
                 ("exists", Value::Bool(false)), ("row", Value::Null),
                 ("group", snapback4_core::value::object([
                     ("__proto__", Value::Null), ("toString", Value::Null), ("value", Value::Int(1)),
-                    ("id", Value::Id("[\"Null\",\"Null\"]".into())),
+                    ("id", Value::Id(snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Null, Value::Null]))),
                 ])),
             ])));
             assert!(matches!(run(&c), Verdict::Pass), "{name}: {:?}", run(&c));

+/-- Retire a due row that physically holds an inserted id. The native engine

+def retireExpiredSameId (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) (t : String)
+    (td : TableDecl) (rid : List Nat) : Except Stop Store :=
+  match findRow (s t) [("id", .id rid)] with
+  | some old =>
+    if expired td now old then refDeleteFrom sch (unexpiredStore sch s now) s t old
+    else pure s
+  | none => pure s

+The SQLite metadata and evaluation-PostgreSQL backend marker
+`maintained_id_version = sha256-v1` bind persisted aggregate targets to that
+function. Opening or adopting either store without the marker — including a
+0.4.28 store whose ids were the unbounded canonical JSON of the group key —
+atomically clears and rebuilds every ordinary aggregate target from its source
+rows, writes the marker, and publishes a new semantic generation. A failure
+rolls back old rows, rebuilt rows, marker and generation together. Adoption
+advice counts the SQLite rebuild even when schema/program bytes are unchanged;
+the evaluation adapter performs the corresponding rebuild under its opening
+writer/head fence. Schemas with ordinary maintains require device capability
 `maintained-id-sha256-v1`; an older device is refused at the handshake before
 it can compute a legacy id and silently read a digest-addressed aggregate as
 zero.

+row in the cascade closure, keeps the expired root physically stored and the
+sweep continues to later due keys. The action walk preflights both kinds of
+blocker before its first set-null or cascade write. The retained root remains
+logically unreadable.
+Short sweeps preserve both a per-table row continuation and a rotating table
+continuation. Repeated bounded calls therefore cannot restart forever at the
+same restricted or read-through-retained roots, and a blocked earlier table
+cannot starve due rows in a later table. After reaching a table's end its row

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/postgres/maintained_identity_upgrade.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/postgres/maintained_identity_upgrade.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..3708ad4a5a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/postgres/maintained_identity_upgrade.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,49 @@
+//! Atomic adoption of the maintained-row address function in the evaluation
+//! adapter. The marker and generation publish in the same writer transaction
+//! as the target rebuild, so no writer can observe mixed legacy/digest ids.
+use super::*;
+
+pub(super) fn install(
+    tx: &store::Tx<'_>,
+    schema: &Schema,
+    creating: bool,
+    generation: u64,
+) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
+    let current = tx.query(
+        "SELECT maintained_identity_version FROM _backend WHERE singleton",
+        &[],
+    )?[0].get::<_, Option<&str>>(0).map(str::to_owned);
+    if current.as_deref() == Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION) {
+        return Ok(generation);
+    }
+
+    let aggregates: Vec<_> = schema.maintains.iter()
+        .filter(|maintain| maintain.private.is_none() && maintain.row_key.is_none())
+        .cloned()
+        .collect();
+    if !creating && !aggregates.is_empty() {
+        let mut runner = Runner::new(
+            tx,
+            schema,
+            Context::system().at(crate::now_ms()),
+            Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort),
+            tx.next_seq()?,
+        );
+        runner.event_free = true;
+        for maintain in &aggregates {
+            runner.rebuild_maintain(maintain)?;
+        }
+    }
+
+    let generation = if creating || aggregates.is_empty() {
+        generation
+    } else {
+        generation.checked_add(1)
+            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))?
+    };
+    tx.execute(
+        "UPDATE _backend SET maintained_identity_version=$1, generation=$2 WHERE singleton",
+        &[&crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION, &store::signed(generation)?],
+    )?;
+    Ok(generation)
+}

+#[test]
+#[ignore = "requires isolated local Postgres; scripts/test-postgres.sh"]
+fn reopening_legacy_maintained_ids_rebuilds_before_publishing_the_generation() {
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), r#"
+table entries:
+  bucket: int
+  public
+view totals: count entries by .bucket
+  public
+"#.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let schema: Schema = serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&bound.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
+    let programs = bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+    let url = std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES").expect("private test-postgres.sh database");
+    let name = format!("sb4_eval_maintained_id_{:x}", crate::ids::seed());
+    let mut engine = PostgresEngine::create(Client::connect(&url, postgres::NoTls).unwrap(), &name, schema, programs).unwrap();
+    engine.load([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("bucket", Value::Int(7))]))]).unwrap();
+    let digest = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let legacy = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let target = engine.schema.tables["totals"].clone();
+    let generation = engine.generation();
+    let tx = engine.store.transaction(Some(WriteMode::Serialized)).unwrap();
+    let old = tx.get("totals", &digest).unwrap().expect("current aggregate");
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &old).unwrap();
+    let mut old = old;
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut old {
+        fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(legacy.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("totals", &target, &old, None).unwrap();
+    tx.execute("UPDATE _backend SET maintained_identity_version=NULL WHERE singleton", &[]).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    drop(engine);
+
+    let mut reopened = PostgresEngine::open(Client::connect(&url, postgres::NoTls).unwrap(), &name).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(reopened.generation(), generation + 1);
+    let tx = reopened.store.transaction(None).unwrap();
+    assert!(tx.get("totals", &legacy).unwrap().is_none());
+    let rebuilt = tx.get("totals", &digest).unwrap().expect("digest aggregate");
+    assert_eq!(rebuilt.field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+
 fn engine() -> PostgresEngine {

         "items": {"columns": {"id": "Id", "key": {"Optional": {"Text": {"max": 100}}}},

 fn run<H: Host>(host: &mut H) {
+    // Digest-addressed maintained rows use the same fixed identity on the
+    // server and each device store, even for the largest composite values in
+    // this generated family.
+    let aggregate = r#"
+table groups:
+  public
+  sync public
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  parent: groups
+  token: text <=64
+  amount: int
+  public
+  sync public
+view totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token
+  public
+"#;
+    let (mut e, b) = compile(aggregate);
+    let principal = "p".repeat(64);
+    let parent = "r".repeat(64);
+    let token = "x".repeat(64);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("groups".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id(&parent))])),
+        ("entries".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+            ("id", id("entry")), ("owner", id(&principal)), ("parent", id(&parent)),
+            ("token", snapback4_core::Value::String(token.clone())),
+            ("amount", snapback4_core::Value::Int(7)),
+        ])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&[
+        id(&principal), id(&parent), snapback4_core::Value::String(token),
+    ]);
+    assert_eq!(expected.len(), 64);
+    let mut d = host.open("aggregate-digest", Some(b)).unwrap();
+    acquire(&mut e, &mut d, &principal, 0);
+    let rows = d.store().raw_rows("totals");
+    assert_eq!(rows.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(rows[0].field("id").unwrap(), &id(&expected));
+    host.close("aggregate-digest", d);
+
     // Server expiry is a physical retirement with the schema's declared
     // referential effects. Devices receive that one committed closure just as
     // they receive an authored parent delete.
@@ -88,10 +128,12 @@ table parents:
   sync public
 table cascadeChildren:
   parent: parents on delete cascade
+  cap 1 by parent
   public 'cascadeChildren'
   sync public
 table nullableChildren:
   parent: parents ? on delete set null
+  cap 1 by parent
   public 'nullableChildren'
   sync public
 "#;

+#[test]
+fn aggregate_identity_generator_checks_bounded_digest_shape_outside_oracle_verdicts() {
+    // Maintained-view evaluation is outside the Lean oracle. Exercise its
+    // physical identity as an explicit generator invariant, so these cases
+    // cannot be counted as oracle passes merely because they are unsupported.
+    use snapback4_core::Value;
+    let mut rng = snapback4_difftest::generate::Rng::new(0x3000_0070_0030_0001);
+    let mut ids = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
+    for n in 0..256 {
+        let key = if n == 0 {
+            vec![]
+        } else {
+            let principal = format!("p{:063}", rng.next());
+            let reference = format!("r{:063}", rng.next());
+            let len = rng.pick(65) as usize;
+            let token = format!("{:016x}", rng.next()).repeat(4)[..len].to_owned();
+            vec![Value::Id(principal), Value::Id(reference), Value::String(token),
+                 Value::Int((rng.next() & ((1 << 53) - 1)) as i64)]
+        };
+        let id = snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&key);
+        assert_eq!(id.len(), 64, "seeded case {n}: {key:?}");
+        assert!(id.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_hexdigit() && !byte.is_ascii_uppercase()));
+        assert!(snapback4_core::value::identity_fits(&id));
+        assert_eq!(id, snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&key));
+        assert!(ids.insert(id), "unexpected digest collision in generated corpus");
+    }
+}

+#[test]
+fn generated_composite_aggregate_groups_keep_fixed_digest_identities() {
+    let source = r#"table parents:
+  insert <- allow
+  read <- allow
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  parent: parents
+  token: text <=64
+  amount: int
+  insert <- allow
+  read <- allow
+view totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token
+  read <- allow
+mutation add(row: id, owner: principal, parent: parents, token: text <=64):
+  insert entries {id: row, owner, parent, token, amount: 1}
+"#;
+    let mut e = engine(source);
+    let principal = "p".repeat(64);
+    let parent = "r".repeat(64);
+    e.seed([("parents".into(), object([("id", Value::Id(parent.clone()))]))]).unwrap();
+    for n in 0..32 {
+        let token = format!("{n:02}{}", "x".repeat(62));
+        let result = e.mutate("add", None, Context::new(&principal, BTreeMap::from([
+            ("row".into(), Value::Id(format!("e{n}"))),
+            ("owner".into(), Value::Id(principal.clone())),
+            ("parent".into(), Value::Id(parent.clone())),
+            ("token".into(), Value::String(token)),
+        ]))).unwrap();
+        assert!(matches!(result.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{result:?}");
+    }
+    let rows = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(rows.len(), 32);
+    for row in rows {
+        let Value::Id(id) = row.field("id").unwrap() else { panic!("aggregate id") };
+        assert_eq!(id.len(), 64);
+        assert!(snapback4_core::value::identity_fits(id));
+        assert_eq!(id, &snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&[
+            row.field("owner").unwrap().clone(), row.field("parent").unwrap().clone(),
+            row.field("token").unwrap().clone(),
+        ]));
+    }
+}
+

diff --git a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Maintained.lean b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Maintained.lean
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..241876980a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Maintained.lean
@@ -0,0 +1,67 @@
+import Quarry.Store
+
+/-!
+# Maintained aggregate identity
+
+The executable engine uses SHA-256 over a domain-separated canonical group
+tuple. This independent model deliberately does not reproduce that
+cryptographic implementation. It models the properties the language and
+store protocols rely on: versioned domain separation, a 64-character
+lowercase-hex identity for every logical group (including the empty tuple),
+and adoption as one state transition that replaces every legacy physical
+identity while publishing the new version.
+-/
+
+namespace Quarry
+
+/-- The protocol domain whose change requires another store capability and
+migration. -/
+def maintainedIdDomainV1 : List Nat := cps "snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1"
+
+/-- A small independent digest abstraction. Its contents are intentionally
+not the engine's SHA-256; only its versioned input and fixed hex shape belong
+to this logical model. -/
+def maintainedIdV1 (key : Key) : List Nat :=
+  (List.range 64).map fun i =>
+    let nibble := (maintainedIdDomainV1.length + (encKey key).length + i) % 16
+    if nibble < 10 then 48 + nibble else 87 + nibble
+
+theorem maintainedIdV1_length (key : Key) : (maintainedIdV1 key).length = 64 := by
+  simp [maintainedIdV1]
+
+/-- The canonical tagged-id overhead is nine bytes and lowercase ASCII is one
+byte per character, so every maintained identity fits the 144-byte id bound. -/
+theorem maintainedIdV1_fits (key : Key) : 9 + (maintainedIdV1 key).length ≤ 144 := by
+  rw [maintainedIdV1_length]
+  omega
+
+def maintainedRowV1 (key : Key) (cols : List (String × Scalar)) : Row :=
+  { id := maintainedIdV1 key, cols }
+
+structure MaintainedState where
+  version : Nat
+  rows : List Row
+
+/-- Adoption rebuilds all reconstructible aggregate rows and publishes the
+identity version in the same pure state result. `old` is an input so the model
+states explicitly that legacy physical ids are never retained. -/
+def adoptMaintainedV1 (_old : MaintainedState)
+    (groups : List (Key × List (String × Scalar))) : MaintainedState :=
+  { version := 1, rows := groups.map fun group => maintainedRowV1 group.1 group.2 }
+
+theorem adoptMaintainedV1_version (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
+    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).version = 1 := rfl
+
+theorem adoptMaintainedV1_ids (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
+    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).rows.map Row.id = groups.map fun group => maintainedIdV1 group.1 := by
+  simp [adoptMaintainedV1, maintainedRowV1]
+
+theorem adoptMaintainedV1_values (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
+    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).rows.map Row.cols = groups.map Prod.snd := by
+  simp [adoptMaintainedV1, maintainedRowV1]
+
+example : (maintainedIdV1 []).length = 64 := maintainedIdV1_length []
+example : (maintainedIdV1 [.id (List.replicate 64 97), .text (List.replicate 64 120)]).length = 64 :=
+  maintainedIdV1_length _
+
+end Quarry

+/-- Apply actions to the physical store using only the rows that are logically
+live as action witnesses. The root may itself be absent from `witnesses`. -/
+def refDeleteFrom (sch : Schema) (witnesses physical : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop Store :=
+  let cl := closure sch witnesses (storeSize sch witnesses + 1) [(t, old)] [(t, old)]
+  if restricted sch witnesses cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
+  else
+    let physical := cl.foldl (fun s (parent, row) => (referencing sch parent).foldl (fun s (child, col, action) =>
+      if action == .setNull then
+        ((witnesses child).filter fun k => names k col row.id && !inClosure cl child k).foldl
+          (fun s k => s.replaceRow child k (clearCol k col)) s
+      else s) s) physical
+    pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) physical)
+

+
+def expired (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool :=
+  match td.expire with
+  | none => false
+  | some c => match r.get c with
+    | .int closes => closes <= now
+    | _ => false
+
+def stored (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
+  (s t).any fun current => current.id == r.id
+

+stops later due keys; cascade and set-null are committed with the root. The
+model has no read-through retained-history rows, so every non-restricted due
+root can be retired immediately. -/
+def sweepExpired (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) : Store :=
+  sch.tables.foldl (fun s td => (s td.name).foldl (fun s r =>
+    if expired td now r && stored s td.name r then
+      match refDeleteFrom sch (unexpiredStore sch s now) s td.name r with
+      | .ok next => next
+      | .error (.refuse "E_REFERENCED") => s
+      | .error _ => s
+    else s) s) s
+

    18	    pub(super) fn apply_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    19	        let schema = self.schema;

    36	            if amount > 0 { self.meter.charge(kind, amount)?; }

    57	    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    58	    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    59	    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    60	        let schema = self.schema;
    61	        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);
    62	        // (Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.)
    63	        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];
    64	        // 3. Close over cascades, breadth first. Every reached row's referencing groups must be held on a device.
    65	        let mut next = 0;
    66	        while next < visits.len() {
    67	            let (table, id) = visits[next].clone();
    68	            next += 1;
    69	            for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
    70	                if self.device && !through_reference(&r) { self.prove_reference_held(&r, &id)?; }
    71	                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
    72	                self.each_child(&r, &id, true, &mut |_, child| {
    73	                    if seen.insert((r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned())) { visits.push((r.child.to_owned(), child)); }
    74	                    Ok(true)
    75	                })?;
    76	            }
    77	        }
    78	        // 4. Restrict: every probe runs, streaming past closure rows (the root
    79	        // included) to the first outside witness; the first occupied reference
    80	        // in schema order is reported. One reference's probes visit each closure
    81	        // row at most once, plus one witness per probe (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
    82	        let mut occupied: Option<(String, String, String)> = None;
    83	        for (table, id) in &visits {
    84	            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
    85	                if r.action != OnDelete::Restrict || through_reference(&r) { continue; }
    86	                let mut outside = false;
    87	                self.each_child(&r, id, false, &mut |_, child| {
    88	                    outside = !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned()));
    89	                    Ok(!outside)
    90	                })?;
    91	                let candidate = (r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned(), table.clone());
    92	                if outside && occupied.as_ref().is_none_or(|o| candidate < *o) { occupied = Some(candidate); }
    93	            }
    94	        }
    95	        if let Some((child, column, parent)) = occupied {
    96	            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    97	            self.refusing_clause(&child, &format!("{column}: {parent} (on delete restrict)"));
    98	            let via = if parent == root_table { String::new() } else { format!(", through a {parent} row this delete cascades to") };
    99	            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED", message: format!("{root_table} at {} is still referenced through {child}.{column} (on delete restrict){via}; delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)", self.statement_site) });
   100	        }

   102	        // child. Decide that blocker before set-null writes begin, so expiry
   103	        // can retain this root and continue without relying on transaction
   104	        // rollback to undo a partial closure.
   105	        for (table, id) in visits.iter().skip(1) {
   106	            if self.physical_children_outside(table, id, &seen)? {
   107	                return Err(self.parent_has_children(table));
   108	            }
   109	        }
   110	        // 5. Set null outside the closure. Each group is gathered whole before
   111	        // any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling that shares the
   112	        // reference's index and move it behind a page cursor. Each row is then
   113	        // reread current before its write.
   114	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — gather, then clear: no row moves out of a walk it has not finished.
   115	        for (table, id) in &visits {

   117	                if r.action != OnDelete::SetNull { continue; }
   118	                let mut group: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   119	                self.each_child(&r, id, true, &mut |_, child| {
   120	                    let child = child.as_str()?.to_owned();
   121	                    if !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.clone())) { group.push(child); }
   122	                    Ok(true)
   123	                })?;

   130	            }
   131	        }
   132	        // 6. Delete the closure, children first, each from its current image.
   133	        let reached: Vec<(String, String)> = visits[1..].iter().map(|(t, id)| Ok((t.clone(), id.as_str()?.to_owned()))).collect::<Result<_, RuntimeError>>()?;
   134	        drop(visits);

   136	            let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, &table, &id))?;
   137	            let Some(current) = current else { continue };
   138	            self.delete_reached(&table, current)?;
