   142	
   143	    /// Streams the ids of the live rows referencing `id` through `r` to
   144	    /// `visit`, through its index, until `visit` answers false. A capped walk
   145	    /// (cascade, set null) reads `PAGE` rows per seek and holds at most the
   146	    /// reference's cap; a restrict probe reads one row per seek, so it examines
   147	    /// nothing past its witness. Images are dropped before `visit` runs. Seeks
   148	    /// start at the live suffix, so an expired backlog is never visited.
   149	    fn each_child(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value, capped: bool, visit: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut Self, Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   150	        let schema = self.schema;
   151	        let index = schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
   152	        let (cap, page_rows) = if capped { (schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).map_or(u64::MAX, u64::from), PAGE) } else { (u64::MAX, 1) };
   153	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — seek on the effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`), so no expired row is visited.
   154	        let ranges = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && !through_reference(r) {
   155	            let now = self.context.now.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
   156	            vec![(vec![id.clone(), Value::Null], None), (vec![id.clone()], Some(Value::Int(now)))]
   157	        } else {
   158	            vec![(vec![id.clone()], None)]
   159	        };
   160	        let mut live = 0_u64;
   161	        for (prefix, gt) in ranges {
   162	            let mut bounds = Bounds { gt, ..Default::default() };
   163	            loop {
   164	                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, r.child, &index, &prefix, &bounds, Dir::Asc, page_rows))?;
   165	                let more = page.len() == page_rows;
   166	                if let Some((_, key)) = page.last() { bounds.after = crate::key::decode(key).map(|v| v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect()); }
   167	                let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(page.len());
   168	                for (row, _) in page {
   169	                    if self.device {
   170	                        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — a device predicts only over current rows: retained history queues the delete, and only a live, current (or own provisional) row is a witness.
   171	                        // A retained, no longer current row is work the reservation does not
   172	                        // price and a witness the device cannot judge: the delete queues.
   173	                        if self.tx.retained_row(r.child, &row)? && !self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? {
   174	                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting meets retained {} history that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
   175	                        }
   176	                        if self.expired(r.table, &row) || !(self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? || self.is_provisional(r.child, &row)?) {
   177	                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs a current image of every {} row that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
   178	                        }
   179	                    } else if self.expired(r.table, &row) { continue; }
   180	                    live += 1;
   181	                    // Activation validates caps and every write keeps them; more than the cap
   182	                    // is a store that broke its own invariant, never a hidden-row count.
   183	                    if live > cap { return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} holds more rows than its cap for one {} row", r.child, r.column, r.column))); }
   184	                    ids.push(row.field("id")?.clone());
   185	                }
   186	                for child in ids {
   187	                    if !visit(self, child)? { return Ok(()); }
   188	                }
   189	                if !more { break; }
   190	            }
   191	        }
   192	        Ok(())
   193	    }
   194	
   195	    /// A device predicts only over a referencing group it holds completely.
   196	    // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — the held-group proof of LLP 3000.007 §20.5, per closure row and reference.
   197	    fn prove_reference_held(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   198	        let index = self.schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
   199	        let (child, key) = (r.child.to_owned(), [id.clone()]);
   200	        // Key-decided readability, and the group itself held (a rejoined or
   201	        // unacquired range is unknown even when the rule admits the viewer).
   202	        if self.device_work(|runner| runner.upsert_absence_proven(&child, &index, &key))? && self.tx.current_group(&child, &index, &key)? { return Ok(()); }
   203	        Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs every {} row that references it held; queued without a local delete", r.child)))
   204	    }
   205	
   206	    /// The engine's own write of a `set null` reference: no rule, every constraint.
   207	    fn clear_reference(&mut self, table: &str, column: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   208	        let mut new = old.clone();
   209	        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut new { fields.insert(column.to_owned(), Value::Null); }
   210	        let patch = crate::value::object([(column, Value::Null)]);
   211	        let outer = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
   212	        let result = self.target_attempt(table, |runner| {
   213	            runner.reserve_maintains(table, &runner.schema.tables[table])?;
   214	            runner.replace_authorized(table, old, new, true, &patch, 0)
   215	        });
   216	        self.engine_action = outer;
   217	        result
   218	    }
   219	
   220	    /// The engine's own delete of a closure row: no rule, its constraints, its
   221	    /// `read through` children's probe, maintains and partition events.
   222	    fn delete_reached(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   223	        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
   224	            runner.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
   225	            let table = &runner.schema.tables[table_name];
   226	            runner.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
   227	            runner.delete_row(table_name, old)
   228	        })
   229	    }
   230	}
   231	
   232	/// Rows a child scan holds per seek: the reservation's transient `4 × row`
   233	/// (LLP 3000.039 §5.1, `Schema::delete_reservation`).
   234	const PAGE: usize = crate::schema::references::REFERENCE_PAGE;
   235	
   236	fn through_reference(r: &Referencing<'_>) -> bool {
   237	    r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column)
   238	}
   239	
   240	/// The closure's rows, children before parents: a table goes first when no
   241	/// other reached table must be removed before it (the cascade and `read
   242	/// through` edges are acyclic by `check`), and within a table, later
   243	/// discoveries first.
   244	fn deletion_order(schema: &crate::schema::Schema, reached: &[(String, String)]) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
   245	    let tables: Vec<&str> = {
   246	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
   247	        for (table, _) in reached { if !seen.contains(&table.as_str()) { seen.push(table.as_str()); } }
   248	        seen
   249	    };
   250	    // edges: child table -> the reached tables it must be removed before
   251	    // (its cascade parents and its `read through` parent; LLP 3000.039 §3).
   252	    let mut edges: Vec<(&str, &str)> = Vec::new();
   253	    for &child in &tables {
   254	        for &parent in &tables {
   255	            if child != parent && schema.deletion_precedes(child, parent) { edges.push((child, parent)); }
   256	        }
   257	    }
   258	    let mut order: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   259	    let mut remaining = tables.clone();
   260	    while !remaining.is_empty() {
   261	        // A table no remaining table references can go now (its referencers are gone).
   262	        let position = remaining.iter().position(|t| !remaining.iter().any(|other| other != t && edges.contains(&(*other, *t))))
   263	            .unwrap_or(remaining.len() - 1);
   264	        order.push(remaining.remove(position));
   265	    }
   266	    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(reached.len());
   267	    for table in order {
   268	        out.extend(reached.iter().rev().filter(|(t, _)| t == table).cloned());
   269	    }
   270	    out
   271	}

   100	
   101	    /// Whether a read-through child remains outside a delete's cascade
   102	    /// closure. Children in `deleting` are removed first by deletion_order and
   103	    /// therefore do not physically retain their parent.
   104	    pub(super) fn physical_children_outside(
   105	        &mut self,
   106	        parent: &str,
   107	        id: &Value,
   108	        deleting: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>,
   109	    ) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   110	        let children: Vec<_> = self
   111	            .schema
   112	            .children(parent)
   113	            .map(|(n, _, _)| n.clone())
   114	            .collect();
   115	        // At most `deleting.len()` matching rows can be skipped across a
   116	        // table: row ids are unique. One more row decides outside occupancy.
   117	        let limit = deleting.len().saturating_add(1);
   118	        // Reserve the complete schema-fixed probe set before its first seek.
   119	        // Never short-circuit on an occupied table, including at low ceilings.
   120	        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the seeks draw from the request's physical allowance; occupancy never selects the logical charge.
   121	        let heap = children.iter().map(|name| super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[name]).saturating_mul(limit as u64)).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
   122	        let probes = (children.len() as u64).saturating_mul(limit as u64);
   123	        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, probes), (MeterKind::Steps, probes), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {
   124	            let mut occupied = false;
   125	            for name in &children {
   126	                let rows = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, limit)?;
   127	                occupied |= rows.into_iter().any(|(row, _)| row.field("id").ok().and_then(|id| id.as_str().ok())
   128	                    .is_none_or(|child| !deleting.contains(&(name.clone(), child.to_owned()))));
   129	            }
   130	            Ok(occupied)
   131	        })
   132	    }

   135	    pub(super) fn refuse_live_children(

   137	        parent: &str,
   138	        id: &Value,
   139	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   140	        let children: Vec<_> = self

   144	            .collect();

   155	        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, children.len() as u64 * 3), (MeterKind::Steps, children.len() as u64 * 3), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {

   202	            if !self.expired(table, &existing) {
   203	                return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} already has a row with id {id}")));
   204	            }

   206	            // disclosed examination (LLP 3000.015 §§1–2).

   211	        if let Some(index) = proof.filter(|_| {
   212	            !if_absent && upsert_index.is_none() && !self.unproved_write && self.timeline_authority.is_none()
   213	        }) {
   214	            // An earlier unknown fact already makes this prediction unproved.
   215	            // Otherwise the proof's own unknown fact is not the prediction's.
   216	            self.unproved_write = self.missing_fact.is_some();
   217	            if !self.unproved_write {
   218	                let beyond = self.beyond_horizon;
   219	                let proved = self.device_work(|runner| runner.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row));

   221	                match proved { Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(_)) => self.unproved_write = true, other => other? }
   222	            }
   223	        }
   224	        // The server's same-id expired replacement preserves occupancy in

   226	        self.check_constraints(table_name, predecessor.as_ref(), &row, Effect::Insert)?;

   232	            runner.tx.put(table_name, table, &row, None)

   239	    pub(super) fn update(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   240	        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
   241	            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
   242	                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),

   249	
   250	    fn update_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   251	        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Update)?;
   252	        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &patch, false)?;
   253	        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &patch)?;
   254	        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &patch)?;
   255	        if patch.as_object()?.contains_key("id") {
   256	            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} update patch cannot change id")));
   257	        }

   261	            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
   262	        })? else {
   263	            return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()]));
   264	        };
   265	        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].sync.is_none() { return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])); }

   267	            && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
   268	        let patch = self.ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(table_name, patch, readable)?;
   269	        let (new, copy) = self.merge_patch(table_name, &old, &patch, readable)?;
   270	        self.replace(table_name, old, new, readable, &patch, copy)

   384	    fn delete_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

   412	    pub(super) fn delete_authorized(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

   422	    /// A delete's own removal of one row, its rules already decided: receipt
   423	    /// membership, the read-through child probe, constraints, maintains and
   424	    /// the partition event. A referential cascade removes each row this way.
   425	    pub(super) fn delete_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

   436	
   437	    /// Remove a row the engine itself retires (an expired one): no rule and
   438	    /// no constraint stands between the engine and its own sweep, while

   440	    pub(super) fn remove(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   441	        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
   442	        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
   443	        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
   444	            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, old)
   445	        })?;

   449	
   450	    /// Physically retire one row whose upper lifetime ended. Expiry bypasses
   451	    /// the root's authored delete rule and constraints, but it does not bypass
   452	    /// the schema's declared effects on rows that reference the root. A
   453	    /// read-through child still retains the expired parent physically.
   454	    // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — expiry cleanup runs the same closure/restrict/set-null actions as an authored delete.
   455	    pub(super) fn retire_expired_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   456	        if self.physical_children(table_name, old.field("id")?)? { return Ok(false); }
   457	        let current = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {
   458	            self.apply_reference_actions(table_name, old)?;
   459	            // A cascade may maintain a column on the root; delete its current
   460	            // physical image so index and cap entries match what is stored.
   461	            let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?;
   462	            self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))?
   463	                .unwrap_or_else(|| old.clone())
   464	        } else {
   465	            old.clone()
   466	        };
   467	        self.remove(table_name, &current)?;
   468	        Ok(true)
   469	    }
   470	
   471	    /// At a program commit's end, retire every synced row the commit changed
   472	    /// and every row it inserted whose lifetime has already ended: its final
   473	    /// image was never live at a commit boundary, so it leaves no row for a
   474	    /// later sweep to announce. Inside the commit the
   475	    /// row existed as written (an insert-then-update still works). Engine
   476	    /// bookkeeping, unmetered: the device's prediction does not do it, and
   477	    /// the program's metering is already recorded. Its work, returned, stays
   478	    /// linear in the rows retired: each aggregate row takes one net change
   479	    /// and each audience key is scanned for a live row once. Seeds and loads
   480	    /// keep their rows for the sweep.
   481	    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — a row whose lifetime ended by its own commit's end never exists at a commit boundary.
   482	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   483	    pub(crate) fn retire_expired_births(&mut self) -> Result<crate::meter::Metered, RuntimeError> {
   484	        let Some(now) = self.context.now else { return Ok(Default::default());
   485	        };
   486	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#3 — an expired final image never grants a new recipient the row's id.
   487	        // A row that existed before the commit and ends it already expired is
   488	        // retired too: accounted from the partitions it was in at the commit's
   489	        // start, it revokes there and announces nothing to a partition it only
   490	        // entered with its expiring image.
   491	        let mut changed: Vec<(String, String)> = self.born_rows.keys()
   492	            .chain(self.touched_rows.keys())
   493	            .filter(|(table, _)| self.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t| t.is_expiring()))
   494	            .cloned().collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect();
   495	        if changed.is_empty() {
   496	            return Ok(Default::default());
   497	        }
   498	        // The log's order must not depend on a map's iteration order.
   499	        let order = self.schema.through_order();
   500	        changed.sort_by_key(|(name,id)| {
   501	            (std::cmp::Reverse(order.iter().position(|n| n == name)),id.clone(),
   502	            )
   503	        });
   504	        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, crate::meter::Meter::new(crate::meter::Ceilings::unbounded(), crate::meter::Cut::Abort));
   505	        self.retiring = Some(Default::default());
   506	        let retired = changed.iter().try_for_each(|(table_name, id)| {
   507	            let Some(row) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))? else { return Ok(());
   508	            };
   509	            let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
   510	            if table.expired(&row, now) { self.retire_expired_row(table_name, &row).map(|_| ()) } else { Ok(()) }
   511	        });
   512	        let aggregates = self.retiring.take().map(|retiring| retiring.aggregates).unwrap_or_default();
   513	        let retired = retired.and_then(|()| self.apply_retired_aggregates(aggregates));
   514	        let work = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, meter).used;
   515	        retired.map(|()| work)
   516	    }
   517	
   518	    /// A column with a declared default takes it when the written row leaves
   519	    /// it out, before validation and before the asset facts, so the row the
   520	    /// rules and the constraints see is the complete one.
   521	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — omitted defaults use the clock and viewer of the host running the insert.
   522	    pub(super) fn with_defaults(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
   523	        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
   524	        if !table.defaults.keys().any(|name| {
   525	            row.as_object().is_ok_and(|fields| !fields.contains_key(name))
   526	        }) {
   527	            return Ok(row);
   528	        }
   529	        let row = row.defaults(table,self.context.now.ok_or_else(||RuntimeError::Input("write clock is not pinned".into()))?,&self.context.viewer)?;
   530	        self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?;
   531	        Ok(row)
   532	    }

     1	import Quarry.Eval
     2	
     3	/-!
     4	# Writes: rule checks and refusals
     5	
     6	From the rules card, `why E_RULE`, `why NOT_FOUND`, `why E_CONFLICT` and the
     7	writes card:
     8	
     9	* **insert** — the insert rule judges the new image (`E_RULE`); a clash on
    10	  `id` or a declared unique key with a hidden row is `E_RULE`; with a visible
    11	  row, `if absent` returns null and an ordinary insert refuses `E_CONSTRAINT`
    12	  or the key's `else CODE`.
    13	* **update** — "readable" means the read rule admits the old row before the
    14	  write; the update rule judges old and next. Denial is `E_RULE` on a readable
    15	  target and `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable one; a missing target is
    16	  `NOT_FOUND`; an `if` condition on an unreadable target is `NOT_FOUND`, and a
    17	  false one on a readable target `E_CONFLICT`; a secondary unique clash is
    18	  `NOT_FOUND` (unreadable target), `E_RULE` (hidden colliding row) or
    19	  `E_CONSTRAINT`. An admitted update may change a hidden row; the result is
    20	  the row if it is readable afterwards, else null.
    21	* **upsert** — patches an existing target as an update; inserts an absent
    22	  one, where an incomplete image or insert-rule denial is `NOT_FOUND`.
    23	* **delete** — `NOT_FOUND` for a missing or unreadable refused target,
    24	  `E_RULE` for a readable one whose delete rule refuses. A row that
    25	  `restrict`, `cascade` or `set null` references name also needs read
    26	  authority (`NOT_FOUND`); then its delete closes over `cascade` references,
    27	  refuses `E_REFERENCED` while a `restrict` reference names a closure row
    28	  from outside the closure, clears `set null` references outside it, and
    29	  removes the closure (LLP 3000.039 §3).
    30	
    31	The order in which checks run when several fail is not documented; the
    32	model's order is OPEN W1–W6.
    33	-/
    34	
    35	namespace Quarry
    36	
    37	def notFound : Stop := .refuse "NOT_FOUND"
    38	def eRule : Stop := .refuse "E_RULE"
    39	def eInput : Stop := .refuse "E_INPUT"
    40	
    41	def toScalarE : Value → Except Stop Scalar
    42	  | .s x => pure x
    43	  | _ => throw eInput
    44	
    45	def scalarsE (fs : List (String × Value)) : Except Stop (List (String × Scalar)) :=
    46	  fs.mapM fun p => do return (p.1, ← toScalarE p.2)
    47	
    48	/-- A column value fits its declaration: only a nullable column takes null. -/
    49	def fits (c : ColDecl) (x : Scalar) : Bool := c.nullable || x != .null
    50	
    51	/-- The new image of an insert. An absent nullable column is null; an absent
    52	required column refuses with `missing`. Unknown fields refuse `E_INPUT`. -/
    53	def buildImage (td : TableDecl) (rid : List Nat) (fields : List (String × Scalar))
    54	    (missing : Stop) : Except Stop Row := do
    55	  if fields.any fun p => p.1 != "id" && !(td.cols.any (·.name == p.1)) then throw eInput
    56	  let cols ← td.cols.mapM fun c => match fields.lookup c.name with
    57	    | some x => if fits c x then pure (c.name, x) else throw eInput
    58	    | none => if c.nullable then pure (c.name, Scalar.null) else throw missing
    59	  return { id := rid, cols }
    60	
    61	/-- The next image of an update: the patch replaces named columns. -/
    62	def patchImage (td : TableDecl) (old : Row) (patch : List (String × Scalar)) : Except Stop Row := do
    63	  if patch.any fun p => !(td.cols.any (·.name == p.1)) then throw eInput
    64	  let cols ← old.cols.mapM fun p => match patch.lookup p.1 with
    65	    | some x =>
    66	      match td.cols.find? (·.name == p.1) with
    67	      | some c => if fits c x then pure (p.1, x) else throw eInput
    68	      | none => throw eInput
    69	    | none => pure p
    70	  return { old with cols }
    71	
    72	def keyOf (cols : List String) (r : Row) : List (String × Scalar) := cols.map fun c => (c, r.get c)
    73	
    74	/-- The first unique key (in order) on which another row (not `except`)
    75	collides with `img`. Keys with a null component never collide (a nullable
    76	unique key permits tied nulls; indexes card). -/
    77	def findClash (rows : List Row) (keys : List (List String)) (img : Row) (except : Option (List Nat)) :
    78	    Option (Nat × Row) :=
    79	  let rec go (i : Nat) : List (List String) → Option (Nat × Row)
    80	    | [] => none
    81	    | k :: ks =>
    82	      let kv := keyOf k img
    83	      if kv.any (·.2 == .null) then go (i + 1) ks
    84	      else match rows.find? (fun r => keyMatch kv r && except != some r.id) with
    85	        | some r => some (i, r)
    86	        | none => go (i + 1) ks
    87	  go 0 keys
    88	
    89	/-- The refusal for a visible clash on unique key `i` of `["id"] ++ uniques`
    90	(`withId`) or of `uniques`. -/
    91	def clashCode (td : TableDecl) (withId : Bool) (i : Nat) : Stop :=
    92	  let j := if withId then i - 1 else i
    93	  if withId && i = 0 then .refuse "E_CONSTRAINT"
    94	  else match td.uniqueCodes[j]? with
    95	    | some (some code) => .refuse code
    96	    | _ => .refuse "E_UNIQUE"
    97	
    98	def findRow (rows : List Row) (ks : List (String × Scalar)) : Option Row :=
    99	  if ks.any (·.2 == .null) then none else rows.find? (keyMatch ks)
   100	
   101	/-- The result of a write: the row if the viewer can read it afterwards,
   102	else null (`why E_CONFLICT`: results are read-rule gets; OPEN W4 for insert). -/
   103	def resultOf (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (t : String) (r : Row) : Value :=
   104	  if readable sch s v t r then r.toValue else .null
   105	
   106	def resultOfAt (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int)
   107	    (t : String) (r : Row) : Value :=
   108	  if readableAt sch s v now t r then r.toValue else .null
   109	
   110	/-- `guardE b e` continues when `b` holds and refuses `e` otherwise. -/
   111	def guardE (b : Bool) (e : Stop) : Except Stop Unit := if b then .ok () else .error e
   112	
   113	def doInsertImage (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int) (t : String) (td : TableDecl)
   114	    (img : Row) (ifAbsent : Bool) (deny clashHidden : Stop) :
   115	    Except Stop (Value × Store) := do
   116	  guardE (td.insert.eval (openedStore sch s now) v { self := some img }) deny
   117	  match findClash (s t) (["id"] :: td.uniques) img none with
   118	  | some (i, r) => do
   119	    guardE (readableAt sch s v now t r) clashHidden
   120	    if ifAbsent then pure (.null, s) else throw (clashCode td true i)
   121	  | none =>
   122	    let s' := s.insertRow t img
   123	    pure (resultOfAt sch s' v now t img, s')
   124	
   125	/-- An update's `if` condition: `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable target,
   126	`E_CONFLICT` when false. -/
   127	def condCheck (rd : Bool) (cond : Option (Row → Except Stop Bool)) (old : Row) : Except Stop Unit :=
   128	  match cond with
   129	  | some f => do
   130	    guardE rd notFound
   131	    let b ← f old
   132	    guardE b (.refuse "E_CONFLICT")
   133	  | none => pure ()
   134	
   135	/-- A secondary unique clash of an update's next image with another row. -/
   136	def clashCheck (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int) (t : String) (td : TableDecl)
   137	    (rd : Bool) (old nw : Row) : Except Stop Unit :=
   138	  match findClash (s t) td.uniques nw (some old.id) with
   139	  | some (i, r) =>
   140	    throw (if !rd then notFound else if !(readableAt sch s v now t r) then eRule else clashCode td false i)
   141	  | none => pure ()
   142	
   143	def doUpdateRow (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int) (t : String) (td : TableDecl)
   144	    (old : Row) (patch : List (String × Scalar)) (cond : Option (Row → Except Stop Bool)) :
   145	    Except Stop (Value × Store) := do
   146	  let nw ← patchImage td old patch
   147	  guardE (td.update.eval (openedStore sch s now) v { old := some old, next := some nw })
   148	    (if readableAt sch s v now t old then eRule else notFound)
   149	  condCheck (readableAt sch s v now t old) cond old
   150	  clashCheck sch s v now t td (readableAt sch s v now t old) old nw
   151	  pure (resultOfAt sch (s.replaceRow t old nw) v now t nw, s.replaceRow t old nw)
   152	
   153	/-- The id of an absent upsert's new row: from the key, else the patch. -/
   154	def upsertId (ks ps : List (String × Scalar)) : Except Stop (List Nat) :=
   155	  match ks.lookup "id" with
   156	  | some (.id x) => pure x
   157	  | _ => match ps.lookup "id" with
   158	    | some (.id x) => pure x
   159	    | _ => throw eInput
   160	
   161	def insertId (fs : List (String × Scalar)) : Except Stop (List Nat) :=
   162	  match fs.lookup "id" with
   163	  | some (.id x) => pure x
   164	  | _ => throw eInput
   165	
   166	/-! ## Referential actions (LLP 3000.039 §3) -/
   167	
   168	/-- Every reference that names table `t` and carries an action:
   169	`(child table, column, action)`. -/
   170	def referencing (sch : Schema) (t : String) : List (String × String × OnDelete) :=
   171	  sch.tables.flatMap fun td => td.cols.filterMap fun c =>
   172	    match c.ref, c.onDelete with
   173	    | some p, some a => if p == t then some (td.name, c.name, a) else none
   174	    | _, _ => none
   175	
   176	/-- Row `k` names the row with id `i` through column `c`. -/
   177	def names (k : Row) (c : String) (i : List Nat) : Bool := k.get c == .id i
   178	
   179	def inClosure (cl : List (String × Row)) (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   180	  cl.any fun p => p.1 == t && p.2.id == r.id
   181	
   182	/-- The rows one step of cascades reaches from `(t, r)`. -/
   183	def cascadeKids (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : List (String × Row) :=
   184	  (referencing sch t).flatMap fun (c, col, a) =>
   185	    if a == .cascade then ((s c).filter fun k => names k col r.id).map fun k => (c, k) else []
   186	
   187	/-- Breadth-first closure over `cascade` references, each row once; `fuel`
   188	bounds the walk (the store's row count suffices). -/
   189	def closure (sch : Schema) (s : Store) : Nat → List (String × Row) → List (String × Row) → List (String × Row)
   190	  | 0, _, acc => acc
   191	  | _ + 1, [], acc => acc
   192	  | f + 1, (t, r) :: rest, acc =>
   193	    let new := (cascadeKids sch s t r).foldl (fun n p => if inClosure (acc ++ n) p.1 p.2 then n else n ++ [p]) []
   194	    closure sch s f (rest ++ new) (acc ++ new)
   195	
   196	/-- Some `restrict` reference names a closure row from outside the closure. -/
   197	def restricted (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (cl : List (String × Row)) : Bool :=
   198	  cl.any fun (t, r) => (referencing sch t).any fun (c, col, a) =>
   199	    a == .restrict && (s c).any fun k => names k col r.id && !inClosure cl c k
   200	
   201	def clearCol (r : Row) (c : String) : Row :=
   202	  { r with cols := r.cols.map fun p => if p.1 == c then (p.1, Scalar.null) else p }
   203	
   204	/-- Clear every `set null` reference that names a closure row from outside it. -/
   205	def setNulls (sch : Schema) (cl : List (String × Row)) (s : Store) : Store :=
   206	  cl.foldl (fun s (t, r) => (referencing sch t).foldl (fun s (c, col, a) =>
   207	    if a == .setNull then
   208	      ((s c).filter fun k => names k col r.id && !inClosure cl c k).foldl
   209	        (fun s k => s.replaceRow c k (clearCol k col)) s
   210	    else s) s) s
   211	
   212	def storeSize (sch : Schema) (s : Store) : Nat := sch.tables.foldl (fun n td => n + (s td.name).length) 0
   213	
   214	/-- The store after deleting `old` from `t` with its referential actions, or
   215	`E_REFERENCED`. With no action-carrying reference into `t`, exactly
   216	`removeRow` (LLP 3000.039 §1). -/
   217	def refDelete (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop Store :=
   218	  let cl := closure sch s (storeSize sch s + 1) [(t, old)] [(t, old)]
   219	  if restricted sch s cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
   220	  else pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) (setNulls sch cl s))
   221	
   222	/-- Apply actions to the physical store using only the rows that are logically
   223	live as action witnesses. The root may itself be absent from `witnesses`. -/
   224	def refDeleteFrom (sch : Schema) (witnesses physical : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop Store :=
   225	  let cl := closure sch witnesses (storeSize sch witnesses + 1) [(t, old)] [(t, old)]
   226	  if restricted sch witnesses cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
   227	  else
   228	    let physical := cl.foldl (fun s (parent, row) => (referencing sch parent).foldl (fun s (child, col, action) =>
   229	      if action == .setNull then
   230	        ((witnesses child).filter fun k => names k col row.id && !inClosure cl child k).foldl
   231	          (fun s witness => match (s child).find? fun current => current.id == witness.id with
   232	            | some current =>
   233	              if names current col row.id then s.replaceRow child current (clearCol current col) else s
   234	            | none => s) s
   235	      else s) s) physical
   236	    pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) physical)
   237	
   238	/-! ## Expiry retirement (LLP 3000.036.003 §6; LLP 3000.039 §3) -/
   239	
   240	def expired (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   241	  match td.expire with
   242	  | none => false

   244	    | .int closes => closes <= now
   245	    | _ => false
   246	
   247	def stored (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   248	  (s t).any fun current => current.id == r.id
   249	
   250	/-- Retire a due row that physically holds an inserted id. The native engine
   251	does this before checking the new image's remaining unique keys, in the same
   252	transaction as the insert. -/
   253	def retireExpiredSameId (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) (t : String)
   254	    (td : TableDecl) (rid : List Nat) : Except Stop Store :=
   255	  match findRow (s t) [("id", .id rid)] with
   256	  | some old =>
   257	    if expired td now old then refDeleteFrom sch (unexpiredStore sch s now) s t old
   258	    else pure s
   259	  | none => pure s
   260	
   261	/-- Retire every due physical row. `restrict` retains that root but never
   262	stops later due keys; cascade and set-null are committed with the root. The
   263	model has no read-through retained-history rows, so every non-restricted due
   264	root can be retired immediately. -/
   265	def sweepExpired (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) : Store :=
   266	  sch.tables.foldl (fun s td => (s td.name).foldl (fun s r =>
   267	    if expired td now r && stored s td.name r then
   268	      match refDeleteFrom sch (unexpiredStore sch s now) s td.name r with
   269	      | .ok next => next
   270	      | .error (.refuse "E_REFERENCED") => s
   271	      | .error _ => s
   272	    else s) s) s
   273	
   274	/-- Apply one write to the store. -/
   275	def applyWrite (cx : Ctx) (s : Store) : RWrite → Except Stop (Value × Store)
   276	  | .insert t fields ifAbsent =>
   277	    match cx.schema.table? t with
   278	    | none => throw eInput
   279	    | some td => do
   280	      let fs ← scalarsE fields

   282	      let img ← buildImage td rid fs eInput
   283	      let s ← retireExpiredSameId cx.schema s cx.now t td rid

     2	//! name it (`on delete restrict | cascade | set null`).
     3	// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — every reference between ordinary durable tables carries one action; restrict is the default.

     7	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8	#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
     9	pub enum OnDelete {
    10	    Restrict,
    11	    Cascade,
    12	    SetNull,
    13	    /// The referencing row stays and names a deleted row (the pre-0.5 meaning),
    14	    /// declared on purpose: history, audit trails, contributions that outlive
    15	    /// their target.
    16	    Keep,
    17	}
    18	
    19	impl OnDelete {
    20	    pub fn words(self) -> &'static str {
    21	        match self { Self::Restrict => "on delete restrict", Self::Cascade => "on delete cascade", Self::SetNull => "on delete set null", Self::Keep => "on delete keep" }
    22	    }
    23	    /// The actions that write the children, bounded by a cap on the reference.
    24	    pub fn writes(self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Cascade | Self::SetNull) }
    25	    /// The actions a delete must consult (every one but `keep`).
    26	    pub fn acts(self) -> bool { self != Self::Keep }
    27	}

    29	/// One action-carrying reference `child.column -> parent`.
    30	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
    31	pub struct Referencing<'a> {
    32	    pub child: &'a str,
    33	    pub table: &'a Table,
    34	    pub column: &'a str,
    35	    pub action: OnDelete,
    36	}
    37	
    38	/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
    39	pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
    40	pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }
    41	
    42	/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
    43	/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
    44	pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
    45	/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
    46	pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;
    47	
    48	impl Schema {
    49	    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    50	    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    51	    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    52	    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
    53	        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
    54	        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
    55	            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
    56	            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    60	    /// (child, column) order.
    61	    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
    62	        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
    63	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    64	        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
    65	            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
    66	                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
    67	                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
    68	                }
    69	            }
    70	        }
    71	        out
    72	    }
    73	
    74	    /// A delete of a `parent` row consults its referencing rows, so it needs
    75	    /// read authority on that row (LLP 3000.039 §3 step 1).
    76	    pub fn is_referenced(&self, parent: &str) -> bool { !self.referencing(parent).is_empty() }
    77	
    78	    /// The index a reference is probed through: its column first, then the
    79	    /// child's deadline column when the child can expire, so one seek skips an
    80	    /// expired backlog. A `read through` reference uses its child index.
    81	    pub fn reference_index(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<&str> {
    82	        let table = self.table(child)?;
    83	        if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) {
    84	            return table.indexes.contains_key(through::CHILDREN).then_some(through::CHILDREN);
    85	        }
    86	        // The effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
    87	        let deadline = table.expiry_column();
    88	        table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
    89	            let columns = index.columns();
    90	            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
    91	                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
    92	        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    93	    }
    94	
    95	    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
    96	    /// or one when the reference itself is a plain unique key. A nullable
    97	    /// reference may have many nulls, but a parent delete probes one non-null
    98	    /// parent id, whose unique group still has at most one row.
    99	    pub fn reference_cap(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<u32> {
   100	        let table = self.table(child)?;
   101	        let declared = table.constraints.iter().find_map(|c| match c {
   102	            Constraint::Cap { by, max } if by.len() == 1 && by[0] == column => Some(*max),
   103	            _ => None,
   104	        });
   105	        let unique = table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns() == [column]).then_some(1);
   106	        declared.into_iter().chain(unique).min()
   107	    }
   108	
   109	    /// Whether `cap` on `table` skips a group whose reference is null: only a
   110	    /// `set null` reference's own cap does (a null reference has no parent).
   111	    pub fn cap_skips_null(&self, table: &Table, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> bool {
   112	        by.iter().zip(group).any(|(column, value)| *value == Value::Null && table.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&OnDelete::SetNull))
   113	    }
   114	
   115	    /// The tables a delete of a `parent` row may write through its writing
   116	    /// actions, and the tables its actions read. A function of the schema, so
   117	    /// a commit's touched set never says which of them a delete reached.
   118	    pub fn action_tables(&self, parent: &str) -> ActionTables {
   119	        let mut out = ActionTables::default();
   120	        let mut queue = vec![parent.to_owned()];
   121	        let mut visited = BTreeSet::new();
   122	        while let Some(table) = queue.pop() {
   123	            if !visited.insert(table.clone()) { continue; }
   124	            for r in self.referencing(&table) {
   125	                out.read.insert(r.child.to_owned());
   126	                if r.action.writes() { out.written.insert(r.child.to_owned()); }
   127	                if r.action == OnDelete::Cascade { queue.push(r.child.to_owned()); }
   128	            }
   129	        }
   130	        // What a write of each of those tables writes in turn: the derived
   131	        // destinations `row_changed` names (aggregate targets, maintained-column
   132	        // owners, row-keyed views).
   133	        let derived: Vec<String> = self.maintains.iter().filter(|m| out.written.contains(&m.source) || m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| out.written.contains(&k.table)))
   134	            .flat_map(|m| [Some(m.target.clone()), m.private.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.table.clone())]).flatten().collect();
   135	        out.written.extend(derived);
   136	        out.written.retain(|name| self.visible(name));
   137	        // Private derivations disappear from the device's projection; their
   138	        // event envelope is exported with each direct source's write price.
   139	        let sources: Vec<String> = out.written.iter().cloned().collect();
   140	        for source in sources {
   141	            if let Some(events) = self.write_reservation(&source).and_then(|plan| plan.action_events) { out.written.extend(events.tables); }
   142	        }

   144	    }
   145	
   146	    /// The order a delete's closure is removed in: a row before any table it
   147	    /// must not outlive — its cascade parent, and its `read through` parent.
   148	    /// A cycle through those edges has no valid order and is refused.
   149	    pub fn deletion_precedes(&self, child: &str, parent: &str) -> bool {

   151	            || self.referencing(parent).into_iter().any(|r| r.child == child && r.action == OnDelete::Cascade)
   152	    }
   153	
   154	    /// A table that reaches itself through cascade and `read through` edges.
   155	    pub fn deletion_cycle(&self) -> Option<String> {
   156	        fn visit<'a>(schema: &'a Schema, name: &'a str, path: &mut Vec<&'a str>, done: &mut BTreeSet<&'a str>) -> Option<String> {
   157	            if path.contains(&name) { return Some(name.to_owned()); }
   158	            if !done.insert(name) { return None; }
   159	            path.push(name);
   160	            for (child, _) in &schema.tables {
   161	                if schema.deletion_precedes(child, name) && let Some(cycle) = visit(schema, child, path, done) { return Some(cycle); }
   162	            }
   163	            path.pop();
   164	            None
   165	        }
   166	        let mut done = BTreeSet::new();
   167	        self.tables.keys().find_map(|name| if self.tables[name].on_delete.values().any(|a| *a == OnDelete::Cascade) || self.is_referenced(name) {
   168	            let mut path = Vec::new();
   169	            visit(self, name, &mut path, &mut done)
   170	        } else { None })
   171	    }
   172	
   173	    /// Whether `name` is a table a timeline orders the writes of.
   174	    pub fn timeline_member(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
   175	        self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name) || t.maps(name) || t.authors.first().is_some_and(|a| a == name))
   176	    }
   177	
   178	    /// The most rows of each table one delete of a `parent` row can reach
   179	    /// through its cascades (its closure), the root included: each cascade
   180	    /// edge multiplies by its cap, and edges into a table add up. `None` when
   181	    /// the cascade graph from `parent` is cyclic or a cascade lacks its cap.
   182	    pub fn closure_bounds(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Vec<(String, u64)>> {
   183	        // Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.
   184	        let mut reach: Vec<String> = vec![parent.to_owned()];
   185	        let mut edges: Vec<(usize, usize, u64)> = Vec::new();
   186	        let mut at = 0;
   187	        while at < reach.len() {
   188	            let table = reach[at].clone();
   189	            for r in self.referencing(&table) {
   190	                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
   191	                let cap = u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?);
   192	                let child = reach.iter().position(|t| t == r.child).unwrap_or_else(|| { reach.push(r.child.to_owned()); reach.len() - 1 });
   193	                edges.push((at, child, cap));
   194	            }
   195	            at += 1;

   197	        // Kahn's order over the reached cascade subgraph; a leftover table is a cycle.
   198	        let mut indegree = vec![0_usize; reach.len()];
   199	        for &(_, child, _) in &edges { indegree[child] += 1; }
   200	        let mut bounds = vec![0_u64; reach.len()];
   201	        bounds[0] = 1;
   202	        let mut ready: Vec<usize> = (0..reach.len()).filter(|&t| indegree[t] == 0).collect();
   203	        let mut done = 0;
   204	        while let Some(table) = ready.pop() {
   205	            done += 1;
   206	            for &(from, child, cap) in edges.iter().filter(|(from, _, _)| *from == table) {
   207	                bounds[child] = bounds[child].saturating_add(bounds[from].saturating_mul(cap));
   208	                indegree[child] -= 1;
   209	                if indegree[child] == 0 { ready.push(child); }

   212	        (done == reach.len()).then(|| reach.into_iter().zip(bounds).collect())

   214	
   215	    /// The fixed charge a delete of a `parent` row reserves before it probes,
   216	    /// cascades or clears any row, over every meter: the closure's rows and
   217	    /// their identities, every restrict probe (one seek per closure row, plus

   280	                    OnDelete::Keep => {}
   281	                }
   282	            }
   283	        }
   284	        // The root is read again, current, before its own removal.
   285	        price[2] = price[2].saturating_add(1);
   286	        // The closure's identities (table and id) are held until the last
   287	        // delete, with the root's and one page of child ids.
   288	        // A set-null group is gathered whole (at most its cap) before it is cleared.

 `restrict`, a read-through child, the meter) aborts the whole mutation, and

-**Expiry is not a delete.** A parent whose `expire at` passes is absent from
-that moment, and the sweep removes it physically; neither fires an action.
-A child of an expiring parent outlives it unless the child declares the same
-lifetime (`read through`, or its own `expire at`). The writes card says so.
+**Expiry is a referential-action trigger.** A parent whose `expire at` passes
+is logically absent at that instant, independent of whether physical cleanup
+can retire it. Every path that physically retires an expired image — scheduled
+sweep, an already-expired birth at commit, and same-id replacement of an
+expired predecessor — applies steps 3–6 above in the same transaction. It
+bypasses only the expired root's authored read/delete authorization; it does
+not bypass `cascade`, `set null`, `restrict`, read-through retention,
+constraints, maintains or partition events on affected rows.
+
+A live `restrict` child keeps the expired root physically stored and the sweep
+continues to later due keys. The retained root remains logically unreadable.
+Short sweeps preserve a per-table scan continuation, so repeated bounded calls
+cannot restart forever at the same restricted or read-through-retained roots;
+after reaching the end they wrap for roots whose blockers were removed.
+Expired children are not live restrict witnesses, as in step 4. A read-through
+child remains the stricter physical exception because its stored image depends
+on its parent. Any error after an earlier cascade or set-null write rolls back
+the whole retirement transaction. A restricted root alone is an expected
+deferred cleanup, not an error for the sweep as a whole.

+#[test]
+fn missing_identity_marker_rebuilds_a_principal_reference_and_max_text_group() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table parents:\n  public\ntable entries:\n  owner: principal\n  parent: parents\n  token: text <=64\n  amount: int\n  public\nview totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    let owner = format!("user:{}", "o".repeat(59));
+    let parent = "p".repeat(64);
+    let token = "t".repeat(64);
+    assert_eq!((owner.len(), parent.len(), token.len()), (64, 64, 64));
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("parents".into(), object([("id", Value::Id(parent.clone()))])),
+        ("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("owner", Value::Id(owner.clone())),
+            ("parent", Value::Id(parent.clone())), ("token", Value::String(token.clone())), ("amount", Value::Int(7))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Id(owner), Value::Id(parent), Value::String(token)]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+    assert_eq!(expected.len(), 64);
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META).unwrap().as_deref(), Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION));
+    let after = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn identity_redeployment_rebuilds_the_ungrouped_empty_tuple() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table entries:\n  public\nview total: count entries\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    e.seed_at([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into()))]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("total").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("$global").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(true));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    let after = e.store().rows("total").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("$global").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(true));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+}

+#[test]
+fn aggregate_identity_capability_upgrades_legacy_wire_and_fences_old_devices() {
+    let schema = compile("table entries:\n  group: text <=64\n  public\nview totals: count entries by .group\n  public\n");
+    assert!(schema.required_capabilities().contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"));
+
+    // A pre-migration stored schema may already have a carrier for unrelated
+    // capabilities. Decode it once, then stamp the digest identity capability
+    // on every newly published schema/projection.
+    let mut legacy = serde_json::to_value(&schema).unwrap();
+    for table in legacy["tables"].as_object_mut().unwrap().values_mut() {
+        for constraint in table["constraints"].as_array_mut().unwrap() {
+            if let Some(names) = constraint.get_mut("Capabilities").and_then(|value| value.as_array_mut()) {
+                names.retain(|value| value != "maintained-id-sha256-v1");
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    let upgraded: Schema = serde_json::from_value(legacy).unwrap();
+    let encoded = serde_json::to_string(&upgraded).unwrap();
+    assert!(encoded.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"));
+
+    let required = snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&upgraded.public_projection());
+    let mut old = snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current();
+    old.version = "0.4.28".into();
+    old.capabilities.retain(|capability| capability != "maintained-id-sha256-v1");
+    let refusal = snapback4_core::device_protocol::check(Some(&old), &required).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_DEVICE_VERSION");
+    assert!(refusal.message.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"), "{refusal:?}");
+}
+

+#[test]
+fn ungrouped_view_is_a_scalar_with_one_hidden_physical_group() {
+    let bound = compile(r#"view total: count posts
+  read <- allow
+  online only
+query totalPosts():
+  return total
+"#);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs[0].result, snapback4_lang::types::Ty::Int);
+    let maintain = &bound.schema.maintains[0];
+    assert!(maintain.by.is_empty());
+    let target = &bound.schema.tables["total"];
+    assert_eq!(target.columns.get("$global"), Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Bool));
+    assert_eq!(target.indexes["byGroup"].columns(), ["$global"]);
+    let encoded = serde_json::to_value(&bound.programs[0].program).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(encoded["body"][0]["return"]["get"]["index"], "byGroup");
+    assert_eq!(encoded["body"][0]["return"]["get"]["key"], serde_json::json!([true]));
+}

    74	    /// Reopen the persisted compiled generation; subsequent transactions refuse
    75	    /// if another connection activates a successor.
    76	    pub fn open(mut client: Client, name: &str) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
    77	        let name = namespace(name)?;
    78	        client.batch_execute(&format!("SET search_path = {name}, pg_catalog;"))?;
    79	        let row = client.query_one("SELECT version,schema,programs FROM _backend WHERE singleton", &[])?;
    80	        if row.get::<_, &str>(0) != store::VERSION {
    81	            return Err(RuntimeError::Store("not this Postgres evaluation store version".into()));
    82	        }
    83	        let schema = decode(row.get(1))?;
    84	        let programs = decode(row.get(2))?;
    85	        supported(&schema, &programs)?;
    86	        // Existing immutable stores adopt this portable admission policy at open.
    87	        // Return one offending index and its exact count, never its row payloads.
    88	        if let Some(row) = client.query(
    89	            "SELECT t, ix, COUNT(*) FROM i WHERE octet_length(k) > $1 GROUP BY t, ix ORDER BY t, ix LIMIT 1",
    90	            &[&(crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES as i32)],
    91	        )?.first() {
    92	            return Err(crate::rows::index_key_refusal(row.get(0), row.get(1), row.get::<_, i64>(2) as u64));
    93	        }
    94	        Self::attach(client, schema, programs, None)
    95	    }
    96	
    97	    // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.3 — read-session storage and all other upgrades
    98	    // commit together, only after every open/adoption check has succeeded.
    99	    fn attach(client: Client, mut schema: Schema, programs: BTreeMap<String, Program>, create: Option<&str>) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
   100	        let mut store = Store::new(client);
   101	        let tx = store.opening_transaction()?;
   102	        tx.batch("SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '5s'; SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s';")?;
   103	        if let Some(name) = create {
   104	            tx.batch(&format!("CREATE SCHEMA {name}; SET search_path = {name}, pg_catalog;"))?;
   105	            tx.batch(store::DDL)?;
   106	            tx.execute("INSERT INTO _backend VALUES (true,$1,$2,$3)",
   107	                &[&store::VERSION, &canonical_json(&schema), &canonical_json(&programs)])?;
   108	        }
   109	        tx.query("SELECT seq FROM _head WHERE singleton FOR UPDATE", &[])?;
   110	        // Admission must use the backend that survived the wait for the head.
   111	        // A format change can leave schema/program bytes unchanged.
   112	        let held = tx.query("SELECT version,schema,programs FROM _backend WHERE singleton", &[])?.remove(0);
   113	        if held.get::<_, &str>(0) != store::VERSION {
   114	            return Err(RuntimeError::Store("not this Postgres evaluation store version".into()));
   115	        }
   116	        let held_schema: Schema = decode(held.get(1))?;
   117	        let held_programs: BTreeMap<String, Program> = decode(held.get(2))?;
   118	        supported(&held_schema, &held_programs)?;
   119	        if held_schema.hash() != schema.hash() || canonical_json(&held_programs) != canonical_json(&programs) {
   120	            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the backend changed while opening; reopen its current generation".into()));
   121	        }
   122	        // Even a no-op ALTER takes an exclusive table lock. Reopening an
   123	        // upgraded store must remain possible while another reader holds it.
   124	        for (column, ddl) in [
   125	            ("generation", "ALTER TABLE _backend ADD COLUMN generation BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1"),
   126	            ("through_shortened", "ALTER TABLE _backend ADD COLUMN through_shortened TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'"),
   127	            ("maintained_identity_version", "ALTER TABLE _backend ADD COLUMN maintained_identity_version TEXT"),
   128	        ] {
   129	            let exists: bool = tx.query("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM pg_attribute WHERE attrelid = '_backend'::regclass AND attname = $1 AND NOT attisdropped)", &[&column])?[0].get(0);
   130	            if !exists { tx.batch(ddl)?; }
   131	        }
   132	        // Repeat admission under the head fence used by physical adoption.
   133	        if let Some(row) = tx.query(
   134	            "SELECT t, ix, COUNT(*) FROM i WHERE octet_length(k) > $1 GROUP BY t, ix ORDER BY t, ix LIMIT 1",
   135	            &[&(crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES as i32)],
   136	        )?.first() {
   137	            return Err(crate::rows::index_key_refusal(row.get(0), row.get(1), row.get::<_, i64>(2) as u64));
   138	        }
   139	        tx.batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _sequences (parent TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, value BIGINT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY(parent, id));")?;
   140	        tx.batch(store::READ_SESSIONS_DDL)?;
   141	        tx.batch("DROP INDEX IF EXISTS _log_row_seq; DROP INDEX IF EXISTS _log_row_seq_kind;")?;
   142	        caps::install(&tx, &schema)?;
   143	        let generation = contribution_upgrade::install(&tx, &mut schema)?;
   144	        let generation = maintained_identity_upgrade::install(&tx, &schema, create.is_some(), generation)?;
   145	        // Retain the adapter's ordinary connection limits on successful open.
   146	        tx.batch("SET statement_timeout = '5s'; SET lock_timeout = '5s';")?;
   147	        tx.commit()?;
   148	        store.generation = generation;
   149	        schema.prepare_delivery_lookup();
   150	        Ok(Self { store, schema, programs, ceilings: Ceilings::default(), mode: WriteMode::Serialized, retries: 0, precharge: Metered::default(), expiry_after: BTreeMap::new() })
   151	    }
   152	
   153	    pub fn schema(&self) -> &Schema {
   154	        &self.schema
   155	    }
   156	    pub fn programs(&self) -> &BTreeMap<String, Program> {
   157	        &self.programs
   158	    }
   159	    pub fn generation(&self) -> u64 {
   160	        self.store.generation
   161	    }
   162	    pub fn set_ceilings(&mut self, ceilings: Ceilings) {
   163	        self.ceilings = ceilings;
   164	    }
   165	    pub fn set_write_mode(&mut self, mode: WriteMode) {
   166	        self.mode = mode;
   167	    }
   168	    /// Evaluation switch: only physical access changes, never the runner.
   169	    pub fn set_prefetch(&mut self, enabled: bool) { self.store.prefetch = enabled; }
   170	    /// Differential evaluation: reserve budget before the first logical charge.
   171	    pub fn set_meter_precharge(&mut self, used: Metered) { self.precharge = used; }
   172	
   173	    pub fn stats(&self) -> Stats {
   174	        Stats { database_calls: self.store.calls.get(), transaction_retries: self.retries,
   175	            prefetch_marked_reads: self.store.marked_reads.get(), prefetch_page_reads: self.store.page_reads.get(), prefetch_rows_transferred: self.store.prefetch_rows.get(),
   176	            prefetch_bytes_transferred: self.store.prefetch_bytes.get() }
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    pub fn query(&mut self, name: &str, context: Context) -> Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal> {
   180	        self.query_with_delivery(name, context, super::DeliveryManifest::Build)
   181	    }
   182	
   183	    pub fn query_with_delivery(&mut self, name: &str, mut context: Context, manifest: super::DeliveryManifest) -> Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal> {
   184	        context.generation = self.generation();
   185	        let program = self.programs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::UnknownOp(name.into()))?;
   186	        if program.kind != ProgramKind::Query {
   187	            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is not a query")).into());
   188	        }
   189	        super::server_query_allowed(&self.schema, name)?;
   190	        validate::validate_inputs(&self.schema, program, &mut context.args)?;
   191	        context.now.get_or_insert_with(crate::now_ms);
   192	        let tx = self.store.transaction(None)?;
   193	        let seq = tx.next_seq()? - 1;
   194	        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, context, Meter::new(self.ceilings, Cut::Cap), seq);
   195	        runner.meter.used = self.precharge;
   196	        let data = runner.run(program)?;
   197	        runner.charge_output(&data)?;
   198	        let (delivery, delivery_meter) = match manifest { super::DeliveryManifest::Build => runner.delivery_manifest(&data), super::DeliveryManifest::Skip => (None, None) };
   199	        let outcome = QueryOutcome { decoration_expiry: runner.decoration_expiry, search_rows: runner.search_rows.take(), search_scopes: None, delivery, delivery_meter, ephemeral: None,
   200	            unavailable: runner.query_unavailable,
   201	            complete: runner.meter.capped_at.is_none()
   202	                && runner.word_cut_at.is_none()
   203	                && !runner.non_uniform
   204	                && !runner.page_overflow
   205	                && !runner.value_source_incomplete
   206	                && !runner.page_position_lost(),
   207	            cut: runner.read_cut(),
   208	            capped_at: runner.meter.capped_at.clone(),
   209	            word_cut_at: runner.word_cut_at.clone(),
   210	            next: runner.outcome_next_cursor()?,
   211	            data,
   212	            seq,
   213	            metered: runner.meter.used,
   214	            tables: runner.read_tables.iter().cloned().collect(),
   215	            next_expiry: runner.next_expiry.get(),
   216	        };
   217	        drop(runner);
   218	        tx.rollback()?;
   219	        Ok(outcome)
   220	    }
   221	
   222	    pub fn replay(&mut self, viewer: &str, op: &str) -> Result<Option<MutationOutcome>, RuntimeError> {
   223	        let tx = self.store.transaction(None)?;
   224	        let receipt = tx.applied(viewer, op)?;
   225	        tx.rollback()?;
   226	        receipt.as_deref().map(MutationOutcome::from_receipt).transpose()
   227	    }
   228	
   229	    /// Compare wire identity before decoding against the installed program.
   230	    // @ref LLP 3000.009#5.1 — an id binds the admitted operation and arguments.
   231	    fn replay_mutation(&mut self, viewer: &str, id: &str, name: &str, args_digest: &str) -> Result<Option<MutationOutcome>, RuntimeError> {
   232	        let tx = self.store.transaction(None)?;
   233	        let receipt = tx.applied_bound(viewer, id, name, args_digest)?;
   234	        tx.rollback()?;
   235	        receipt.as_deref().map(MutationOutcome::from_receipt).transpose()
   236	    }

   181	        if outcome.is_ok() {

   187	    }
