873-read rule. Writing `exists members[.message.conversation, viewer]` repeats a parent traversal, not a local field: when it exactly repeats the parent rule, `check --fix` replaces that whole read line with an inheritance comment. Dropping `.message` would change its meaning. An unpinned aggregate scan still refuses `E_READ_THROUGH_SCAN`.
874-The aggregate group-key index lives on the server: this nested page uses server
875-fallback on devices, even if they hold all child rows. Complete-key scalar totals
876:can still recount held children locally. The builder's separate raw-reaction
877-query remains suitable for offline reaction UI. Missing parent/source coverage
878:is unknown; expired or retained history does not contribute to current totals. All pages and recounts share
879-the operation's ordinary meter.

2a30c75480:snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/write.rs:880:    pub(super) fn row_changed(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Option<&Value>, new: Option<&Value>, origin: Origin) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
use snapback4_core::{engine::{Context, Engine, WriteState}, value::Value};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

const MODEL: &str = include_str!("fixtures/maintained-columns.q");

fn compile(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",bound.diagnostics);
    bound
}
fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
    let bound = compile(source);
    Engine::memory(bound.schema,bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()
}
fn id(s: &str) -> Value { Value::Id(s.into()) }
fn context(args: &[(&str,Value)]) -> Context { Context::new("alice",args.iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.to_string(),v.clone())).collect()).at(1000) }
fn mutate(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, args: &[(&str,Value)]) -> Value {
    let outcome = e.mutate(op,None,context(args)).unwrap();
    match outcome.state { WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => result, other => panic!("{op}: {other:?}") }
}
fn post(e: &mut Engine, name: &str, at: i64) {
    let row = mutate(e,"post",&[("id",id(name)),("at",Value::Int(at))]);
    for field in ["likeCount","selected","total"] { assert!(row.field(field).is_err()); }
    assert_eq!(row.field("id").unwrap(),&id(name));
}
fn like(e: &mut Engine, name: &str, parent: Value, active: bool, amount: i64) {
    mutate(e,"like",&[("id",id(name)),("postId",parent),("active",Value::Bool(active)),("amount",Value::Int(amount))]);
}
fn totals(e: &mut Engine, parent: &str) -> (i64,i64,i64) {
    let row = e.query("postRow",context(&[("id",id(parent))])).unwrap().data;
    let n = |field|row.field(field).unwrap().as_int().unwrap();
    (n("likeCount"),n("selected"),n("total"))
}

#[test]
fn oracle_moves_filters_sums_birth_and_last_delete() {
    let mut e = engine(MODEL);
    let mut model = BTreeMap::<String,(String,bool,i64)>::new();
    like(&mut e,"early",id("p"),true,7);
    model.insert("early".into(),("p".into(),true,7));
    post(&mut e,"p",1); post(&mut e,"q",2);
    let check = |e: &mut Engine, model: &BTreeMap<String,(String,bool,i64)>| {
        for p in ["p","q"] {
            let rows: Vec<_> = model.values().filter(|(parent,_,_)|parent == p).collect();
            let expected = (rows.len() as i64,rows.iter().filter(|(_,a,_)|*a).count() as i64,rows.iter().filter(|(_,a,_)|*a).map(|(_,_,n)|n).sum());
            assert_eq!(totals(e,p),expected,"parent {p}");
        }
    };
    check(&mut e,&model);
    for n in 0..8 {
        let name = format!("l{n}");
        let p = if n % 2 == 0 { "p" } else { "q" };
        like(&mut e,&name,id(p),n % 3 == 0,n-4);
        model.insert(name,(p.into(),n % 3 == 0,n-4));
        check(&mut e,&model);
    }
    mutate(&mut e,"change",&[("id",id("early")),("postId",id("q")),("active",Value::Bool(false)),("amount",Value::Int(100))]);
    model.insert("early".into(),("q".into(),false,100));
    check(&mut e,&model);
    for name in model.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>() { mutate(&mut e,"unlike",&[("id",id(&name))]); model.remove(&name); check(&mut e,&model); }
}

#[test]
fn clique_pages_match_independent_count_time_model() {
    let mut e = engine(MODEL);
    let mut counts = BTreeMap::new();
    for (p,at) in [("a",10),("b",30),("c",20),("d",40),("e",5),("f",20)] { post(&mut e,p,at); counts.insert(p,(0,at)); }
    let check = |e: &mut Engine, counts: &BTreeMap<&str,(i64,i64)>| {
        let mut expected: Vec<_> = counts.iter().map(|(p,(n,at))|(*n,*at,p.to_string())).collect();
        expected.sort(); expected.reverse();
        let mut cursor = Value::Null;
        let mut actual = Vec::new();
        loop {
            let page = e.query("ranked",context(&[("c",cursor)])).unwrap();
            if let Value::Array(rows) = page.data { for row in rows.iter() { actual.push((row.field("likeCount").unwrap().as_int().unwrap(),row.field("at").unwrap().as_int().unwrap(),row.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap().to_owned())); } }
            let Some(next) = page.next else { break };
            cursor = Value::String(next);
        }
        assert_eq!(actual,expected);
    };
    check(&mut e,&counts);
    for (n,p) in ["a","c","b","a","c","d"].iter().enumerate() {
        like(&mut e,&format!("l{n}"),id(p),true,1); counts.get_mut(p).unwrap().0 += 1; check(&mut e,&counts);
    }
    mutate(&mut e,"unlike",&[("id",id("l0"))]); counts.get_mut("a").unwrap().0 -= 1; check(&mut e,&counts);
    mutate(&mut e,"deletePost",&[("id",id("c"))]); counts.remove("c"); check(&mut e,&counts);
}

#[test]
fn writes_and_result_fields_refuse_and_brand_stays_a_write_key() {
    for body in [
        "insert posts { at: 0, likeCount: 0 }",
        "insert posts { at: 0, likeCount: 0 } if absent",
        "update posts['p'] { likeCount: 0 }",
        "upsert posts['p'] { at: 0, likeCount: 0 }",
    ].into_iter().flat_map(|body| ["likeCount","selected","total"].map(|field| body.replace("likeCount",field))) {
        let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),format!("{MODEL}\nmutation bad():\n  {body}\n"))]);
        assert!(b.programs.iter().all(|p|p.program.name != "bad"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.code == "E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN"),"{body}: {:?}",b.diagnostics);
    }

  likeCount: count likes by postId
  selected: count likes where .active by postId
  total: sum likes of amount where .active by postId
  by byLikes: likeCount, at, id
  public
  insert <- allow
  delete <- allow
  online only
table likes:
  postId: posts ? on delete keep
  active: bool
  amount: int
  public
  insert <- allow
  update <- allow
  delete <- allow
  online only
mutation post(id: id, at: time):
  return insert posts { id, at }
mutation like(id: id, postId: posts ?, active: bool, amount: int):
  insert likes { id, postId, active, amount }
mutation change(id: likes, postId: posts ?, active: bool, amount: int):
  update likes[id] { postId, active, amount }
mutation unlike(id: likes):
  delete likes[id]
mutation deletePost(id: posts):
  delete posts[id]
query postRow(id: posts):
  return posts[id]
query ranked(c: cursor ?):
  return posts after c last 2 by byLikes { id, at, likeCount }
## Writes

**W1. In what order does an insert run its checks?**
- *Model:* the insert rule runs first (`E_RULE`). Then collisions are
  checked: `id` first, then each declared unique key in order. A hidden clash
  refuses `E_RULE`. A visible clash refuses `E_CONSTRAINT` or the key's
  `else CODE`, or returns null under `if absent`.
- *Consequence:* an `if absent` insert that its rule denies refuses `E_RULE`
  even when it would clash visibly.
- *Documents:* each outcome is documented. Their precedence is not.
- *Engine (observed):* agrees on all 1,790 compared inserts (insert rule
  allow, deny, owner or witness; the inserted id clashing with a visible or
  hidden row about half the time).

**W2. What does an insert return when its writer cannot read the new row?**
- *Documents:* the documents say results are read-rule gets for update and
  upsert (`why E_CONFLICT`). A sequence insert by a writer who cannot read
  the new row refuses `E_RULE` (writes card).
- *Model:* the result is the row if the writer can read it after the write,
  and null otherwise, with no refusal.

**W3. In what order does an update run its checks?**
- *Model:*
  1. patch validation (`E_INPUT`);
  2. the update rule: `E_RULE` on a readable target, `NOT_FOUND` on an
     unreadable one;
  3. the `if` condition: `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable target, `E_CONFLICT`
     on a readable one;
  4. secondary unique clashes.
- *Documents:* each outcome is documented. Their precedence when several
  fail is not.
- *Engine (observed):* agrees on every compared `update` (1,788) and on every
  `upsert` (1,745) except the 77 R2 cases, over readable and unreadable targets
  (`E_RULE` against `NOT_FOUND`), missing targets and unique-key clashes. What does the insert arm of an absent upsert refuse?**
- *Documents:* an incomplete image and insert-rule denial are both
  `NOT_FOUND`.
- *Model:* a clash with a hidden row is also `NOT_FOUND`. A clash with a
  visible row is `E_CONSTRAINT` or the key's code. The documents are silent
  on both clash cases.
- *Engine (observed):* agrees on the absent-target upserts generated (a
  missing key; the generator's unique `tag` value never clashes, so the clash
  cases are not yet exercised).
- *Engine (observed, widened generator, seeds 0–19,999, 2026-10-08):* block
  and prelude writes now put a seeded row's `tag`. On a clash with a row the
  viewer **cannot** read, the engine refuses `E_RULE` ("a unique key ...
  contains a row you cannot read") where the model refuses `NOT_FOUND`
  (`lean--prelude-code--E_RULE~NOT_FOUND`, 24 cases, and
   its row count from the request's physical authorization allowance, and
   publish the schema-fixed trace of §4.2.
3. **Close.** Starting from `p`, follow every `cascade` reference to the
   live rows that reference a reached row, and repeat: the **closure** `S`
   is `p` plus every row reached. (`S` is finite: the cascade graph is
   acyclic and each edge has an explicit or inferred effective bound.) Steps 3–5 stream the referencing rows
   through the reference index (§2.3) a bounded page at a time and keep only
   identities; no image outlives its page.
4. **Restrict.** For every row `s` in `S` and every `restrict` reference
   into `s`'s table, look for a live row that references `s` **and is not
   itself in `S`**: the probe streams past the rows of `S` that name `s` —
   `p` included, which may name itself — to the first live row outside `S`
   (each row of `S` names one target per reference, so a delete's probes
   visit each row of `S` at most once, plus one witness per probe). Every
   such probe runs. If any is occupied, refuse with
   the **first occupied reference in schema order** of
   `(child table, column)` — never the first found while walking hidden
   rows:

   `E_REFERENCED: projects at <site> is still referenced through comments.task (on delete restrict); delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)`

   "Live" means present and not expired. A row inserted earlier in the same
   transaction counts; one deleted earlier in it does not. Because rows in
   `S` are excluded, a diamond (`A` cascades to `B` and `C`, and `C`
   restricts `B`) or a self-referencing child table deletes when the whole
   closure goes together, as SQL's statement-end `NO ACTION` does. A `read
   through` reference is not part of this pass: it keeps its own refusal,
   `E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN`, and its stricter physical-child probe (LLP
   3000.014 §3.3), evaluated as each row of `S` is removed in step 6. When
   both would refuse, `E_REFERENCED` comes first (step 4 runs before any
   removal).
5. **Set null.** For every row `s` in `S` and every `set null` reference into
   `s`'s table, clear the column of each live referencing row **not in
   `S`** (deletion dominates: a row that is going away is not rewritten).
   Each reference's group is gathered whole (ids only, at most its effective
   bound: the explicit cap or inferred one)
   before any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling whose
   maintained column shares the reference's index and move it behind a page
   cursor. The write rereads the row's current image immediately before clearing it
   (an earlier clear's maintained write may have changed it), consults no
   rule, and applies its constraints, maintains and partition events as any
   write does.
6. **Delete.** Remove the rows of `S` other than `p`, each table before every
   table it must not outlive: its cascade parents and its `read through`
   parent. Only those edges order the removal (an ordinary `restrict` or
   `set null` reference between rows of `S` imposes none, since step 4
   already cleared them), `check` refuses a cycle in their union
   (`E_REFERENCE_ACTION`; e.g. `B` cascades from `P`, `C` cascades from `B`,
   and `B` reads through `C`), and ties keep discovery order. Each row is
   removed from its current image, consulting no rule and applying
   constraints, maintains and partition events as any delete does. Then
   remove `p` as today, **from its current image too**: the actions may have
   changed it (a maintained column on `p` mirrors its children's removal), and
   a store removes index entries from the image it is given. Authorization
   stays the decision of step 1.

Every step is inside the mutation's transaction: a refusal anywhere (a
`restrict`, a read-through child, the meter) aborts the whole mutation, and
nothing commits.

**Expiry is a referential-action trigger.** A parent whose `expire at` passes
is logically absent at that instant, independent of whether physical cleanup
can retire it. Every path that physically retires an expired image — scheduled
sweep, an already-expired birth at commit, and same-id replacement of an
expired predecessor — applies steps 3–6 above in the same transaction. It
bypasses only the expired root's authored read/delete authorization; it does
not bypass `cascade`, `set null`, `restrict`, read-through retention,
constraints, maintains or partition events on affected rows.

A live `restrict` child, or a physically retained read-through child outside
the cascade closure but below a row in it, keeps the expired root physically

I found a runtime interaction in same-ID replacement: the replacement’s maintained columns are populated before retirement cascades remove the old row’s children, so it can be inserted with a stale count. I’m checking the final line references and separating the runtime findings from the Lean-model discrepancies.

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
struct Table {
    name: String,
    cols: Vec<Col>,
    uniques: Vec<Vec<String>>,
    codes: Vec<Option<String>>,
    /// Caps are carried as unenforced declarations: a program that inserts
    /// into or updates a capped table is outside the fragment (`table-cap`).
    caps: Vec<(u32, Vec<String>)>,
}

impl Table {
    fn col(&self, name: &str) -> Option<Kind> {
        if name == "id" {
            return Some(Kind::Id(IdSort::Ref(self.name.clone())));
        }
        self.cols.iter().find(|c| c.name == name).map(|c| c.kind.clone())
    }
}

/// A checked program set, translated.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
fn table_shape(t: &ast::TableDecl) -> R<Table> {
    for (present, what) in [
        (t.receipts.is_some(), "receipts"),
        (t.ephemeral.is_some(), "ephemeral"),
        (!t.search.is_empty(), "search"),
        (!t.immutable.is_empty(), "immutable"),
        (!t.final_exists.is_empty(), "after-insert"),
        (t.retain.is_some(), "retain"),
    ] {
        if present {
            return no(&format!("table-{what}"), format!("table {} declares {what}", t.name));
        }
    }
    let mut cols = Vec::new();
    for c in &t.columns {
        for (present, what) in [
            (c.sequence.is_some(), "sequence"),
            (c.written_by.is_some(), "written-by"),
            (c.readable_by_audience, "audience-field"),
        if independent && !authorized {
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.
            // Only still-pending checks belong in this preflight allowance.
            let insert_checks = if authorized { 0 } else { 1 + u64::from(upsert_index.is_some() && supplied_sequence.is_ok()) };
            self.reserve_sequence_authorization(table, &row, insert_checks)?;
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — an unreadable allocation cannot expose counter,
        // uniqueness or cap state, even to a principal allowed to insert.
        if !self.sequence_insert_readable(table_name, &row)? {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "sequence read authority");
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "sequence read authority") });
        }
        if let Err(error) = supplied_sequence {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — absent upserts decide insert authority before field diagnostics.
            // A target key may supply an ordinal even when the patch does not.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
                return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
            }
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
        }

NOT READY

1. **Same-ID replacement can persist stale maintained columns.** [write.rs:207](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/write.rs:207) retires the predecessor after `birth_columns` populated the replacement at line 140. If an expired parent has one child contributing to a maintained count and retirement cascades that child away, the replacement still contains count `1`. The private total becomes zero, but mirroring skips the expired predecessor; nothing refreshes the replacement before insertion.

2. **The expanded physical-child probe exceeds its enclosing reservation.** [through.rs:123](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/through.rs:123) reserves closure-size-plus-one images, but it runs inside `apply_reference_actions` with an unbounded replacement meter and `reference_prepaid = true`. Those charges are discarded when the original meter returns. [references.rs:255](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/references.rs:255) still prices only the prior three-seek lifetime hook. A large closure with wide, uncapped outside read-through children can therefore materialize far more data than the admitted heap and physical-work reservation covers.

3. **Ordinary Lean deletes still treat expired children as action witnesses.** [Write.lean:321](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Write.lean:321) calls `refDelete` on the full physical store. An unexpired parent with an expired, unswept restrict child consequently refuses `E_REFERENCED` in Lean, while runtime reference scans exclude that child and permit deletion. Expired cascade and set-null children likewise receive different treatment.

4. **Lean mutations omit commit-end expiry retirement.** [Run.lean:76](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Run.lean:76) commits the statement evaluator’s store directly. A supported mutation inserting an already-due root and a live restrict child commits both in Lean; the runtime’s `retire_expired_births` refuses `E_REFERENCED` and rolls everything back. Cascade and set-null effects on born-expired roots are also omitted.

5. **Formal same-ID retirement changes insert authorization timing.** [Write.lean:283](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Write.lean:283) retires the predecessor before `doInsertImage` evaluates the insert rule. Runtime evaluates that rule before retirement. If a live child both authorizes insertion through an `exists` rule and cascades from the expired predecessor, runtime authorizes then replaces successfully, whereas Lean removes the witness first and refuses `E_RULE`. The upsert insert arm has the same discrepancy.
